## [RELOAD: Retrofitted Loops Add Adaptive Test-Time Compute to Typed Decision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07736) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.07736) introduces RELOAD, a method that fine-tunes pretrained encoders or decoders with a damped loop over their middle block and uses a lightweight controller to adaptively choose the number of passes per input. Across six models from 0.4B to 4B parameters, looping improves accuracy and calibration, with encoders gaining up to 23 points on out-of-distribution reasoning tasks that require longer chains of reasoning than seen in training. Typed decision models cannot spend more test-time compute on hard inputs by generating more tokens, so RELOAD offers a parameter-free way to allocate adaptive latent computation. This could benefit the ML efficiency and reasoning community by showing that retrofitted loops, when trained jointly, close a gap that neither matched training compute nor more data can close. The gains are mostly on problems needing longer reasoning chains than any seen in training, and separate layers for each pass come close only with nearly twice the parameters. The controller retains most of the gain at about three quarters of the compute, though this is a single arXiv preprint without community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Test-time compute refers to the additional computation a model performs during inference, which generative language models can scale by generating more tokens for hard inputs. Typed decision models instead choose among a fixed set of answer options in a single forward pass, so they lack this ability. Looping a block of layers offers extra computation in latent space without adding parameters, but prior work found no gain from loops retrofitted into conventionally pretrained models.

## [Scaling Law for SAE Training Data Shows Quality Plateau](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07756) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.07756) derives a scaling law for the number of unique activations needed in sparse autoencoder (SAE) training, showing that quality improves rapidly with buffer size but plateaus, after which replaying the buffer is nearly as effective as using fresh activations. The authors validate this across dictionary sizes, token budgets, layers of Llama-3.1 and Qwen3, and BatchTopK, TopK, and JumpReLU SAEs, finding that choosing the smallest buffer that reaches the plateau reduces activation storage by 8–64× with negligible quality loss. Activation storage is a major bottleneck in SAE training, so this practical rule could significantly lower the cost and infrastructure burden of interpretability research on large language models. It provides actionable guidance for researchers training SAEs at scale, potentially accelerating work in mechanistic interpretability. The scaling law describes the tradeoff between diversity and repetition under a fixed training budget, and is evaluated using explained variance, reconstruction MSE, feature absorption, automated interpretability, and sparse probing. Sweeps with mathematical text and Python code test the effect of changing the activation source, confirming the plateau holds across data types.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are a popular tool in mechanistic interpretability for decomposing LLM activations into human-interpretable features. Training SAEs requires storing many unique activation vectors from the model, which can be a significant storage bottleneck. Scaling laws describe how model performance changes with resources like data or compute, and this paper applies that concept to the size of the activation buffer in SAE training.

## [Axis-LoRA: Per-Axis LoRA Adapters Boost Personal-Memory Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07820) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Axis-LoRA introduces four LoRA branches inside embedding models, each dedicated to time, place, person, or event, combined via a per-query gate. On LongMemEval, ATM-Bench, and LoCoMo, it improves mean R@10 by 1.28–1.56 percentage points across three standard embedding models while training only a small fraction of base parameters. Personal-memory retrieval is critical for LLM personalization, but frozen embedding models fail to distinguish memories along key axes. Axis-LoRA offers a parameter-efficient fix that could improve recall in AI assistants and memory systems without retraining entire models. The method trains on a small axis-counterfactual dataset and uses a per-query gate to combine the four LoRA branches. Gains are measured as mean R@10 over four benchmarks, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects small trainable low-rank matrices into Transformer layers. Embedding models convert text into dense vectors for retrieval, but they often fail to capture fine-grained distinctions like time or place. LongMemEval is a benchmark for long-term memory in chat assistants, testing abilities such as information extraction and temporal reasoning.

## [Agentic LLMs Could Finally Realize the 2003 Knowledge Plane for Network Management](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07827) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv position paper argues that agentic large language models can finally unlock the Knowledge Plane vision proposed by Clark et al. in 2003, which originally required reasoning and unstructured communication capabilities that the technology of the day lacked. The paper outlines new research challenges that arise in decentralized, multi-agent, coopetitive network management environments. This work connects a foundational networking concept with modern AI agent capabilities, potentially enabling automated cross-domain network management that has remained largely manual for two decades. It could influence future research in AI-driven networking and multi-agent systems, affecting network operators, AI researchers, and standards bodies. The paper is a position paper without empirical results, and it specifically highlights research problems in coopetitive settings where network domains simultaneously cooperate and compete. It notes that automated network management has remained confined to single domains, with interdomain problems still resolved by human operators.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: In 2003, Clark et al. proposed the Knowledge Plane, a pervasive system within the network that builds and maintains high-level models of what the network is supposed to do, in order to provide services and advice to other network elements. It envisioned intelligent agents sharing rich information about network state to collaboratively manage the Internet across administrative boundaries. However, the technology at the time lacked the reasoning and unstructured communication capabilities needed, so the vision was not realized. Agentic large language models combine LLM reasoning with the ability to act and interact in the real world, which the paper argues can finally provide these missing capabilities.

## [THG-Omni: Graph Supervision for Traceable Video Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07836) ⭐️ 7.0/10

THG-Omni introduces a human-centric graph-supervision framework that links SAM3 participant tracks with temporally grounded Qwen3-VL annotations to teach omni-modal models to reason over structured, traceable video evidence. On NExT-QA, it achieves 89.54% accuracy with the matched graph versus 85.10% without graph input. This work addresses a key gap in video question answering: models often give answers without showing which participant, event, or interval supports them. By grounding evidence in identifiable participants and events, THG-Omni could improve trust and interpretability in human-centric video reasoning, benefiting applications like social behavior analysis and deception detection. The framework uses SAM3 for participant tracking and Qwen3-VL for temporally grounded annotations, with candidate semantic claims verified against source video and audio before constructing training questions. Experiments show consistent improvements on IntentBench, and a compact graph input achieves 78.22% accuracy compared to 78.02% with the full graph and 77.58% without graph input.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Omni-modal models can process video, audio, and text, but their reasoning is often opaque. SAM3 (Segment Anything Model 3) is a unified foundation model for promptable segmentation and tracking in images and videos using text or visual prompts. Qwen3-VL is a powerful vision-language model series that provides strong visual understanding. THG-Omni combines these tools to create graph-based supervision that makes video reasoning traceable.

## [LLMs Audit Clinical Prediction Networks via Computation, Not Weight Access](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07847) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.07847) reports pre-specified simulation experiments showing that GPT-6 Astra and four other GPT models can audit clinical prediction networks—recovering planted relationships, avoiding false alarms, and detecting biased subgroups—when given a calculation-only tool, even without direct access to raw weights. Without tools, Astra succeeded only on small networks (353 weights) but became unreliable at 3,457 weights, and under a default instruction it declined 11 of 12 audit and repair tasks as unidentifiable. This finding suggests that LLM-based auditing of clinical prediction models may not require weight access, lowering barriers for safety and interpretability audits in healthcare and potentially enabling black-box auditing of proprietary models. It also highlights the importance of elicitation (explicit instructions) and computation tools in enabling reliable LLM auditing. The study used Tanh networks ranging from 105 to 38,401 weights trained on simulated subarachnoid haemorrhage cohorts, with some networks containing planted relationships or biased labels. With the calculation tool, Astra recovered planted relationships in all 29 runs on networks up to 38,401 weights, raised no false alarms in 15 null-network runs, and found the biased subgroup in 6 of 6 runs; black-box access performed as well as white-box access, and a well-designed script matched the best LLM repair.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have recently been shown to read and edit the weights of small neural networks, suggesting they could audit clinical prediction models directly from files. However, prior demonstrations conflated reading raw weights with executing code, making it unclear whether weight access itself is necessary. This paper separates these conditions to isolate the role of computation. Clinical prediction models are neural networks trained on patient data to predict outcomes like death, and auditing them involves checking for planted relationships or biases.

## [CORE: Training-Free Harness for Deep Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07859) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CORE introduces a training-free harness that combines coarse-to-fine retrieval with context management to return concise, relevant snippets for deep research agents. It achieves state-of-the-art answer accuracy and evidence recall in controlled closed-search comparisons with the Tongyi DeepResearch and OpenResearcher harnesses across three agent models and three benchmarks. Deep research agents face a trade-off between retrieving long passages for reliable matching and keeping the agent's context short to avoid bloat. CORE resolves this by returning only the most relevant snippet from each passage, potentially improving both accuracy and efficiency for long-horizon information-seeking tasks. CORE uses a dense retriever for coarse passage retrieval, then reuses token-level similarities from a late-interaction reranker to locate the most relevant snippet within each passage at no additional encoding cost. Context management allows the agent to expand context around already-retrieved sources and suppresses repeated snippets, with evaluations on corpora containing up to 553 million documents.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Deep research agents answer complex questions by repeatedly calling search tools, and the harness that runs these calls must return evidence that stays in the agent's context for the rest of the trajectory. Dense retrievers and late-interaction rerankers like ColBERT are common components: the former matches passages semantically, while the latter computes token-level similarities between query and document for fine-grained relevance. Coarse-to-fine retrieval first narrows down candidate passages and then refines within them, balancing recall and precision.

## [InnerLogic-Surg: Open Surgical Vision Encoders via Continual Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07883) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InnerLogic-Surg introduces an open family of surgical vision encoders (21M–300M parameters) created by continually pretraining a transformer on millions of surgical images from 11,965 internet-sourced videos spanning ~2,900 hours and over 15 procedure types. The largest model achieves the best linear-probe performance across all 20 surgical tasks in a benchmark covering workflow, instrument and anatomy recognition, segmentation, detection, and monocular depth. This work demonstrates that surgical continual pretraining yields transferable representations that generalize across procedures, anatomical regions, and task types, offering a scalable alternative to procedure-specific models that have traditionally dominated surgical computer vision. It provides a reusable, open backbone that could broadly enhance surgical image analysis without requiring large task-specific datasets. The training recipe inherits DINOv3's pretraining stage to preserve representation ability after surgical adaptation, and adds a learned feature-upsampling module for high-resolution dense prediction at low token cost. The models range from 21M to 300M parameters, and the benchmark covers 20 tasks under the same architecture.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Vision foundation models are large neural networks pretrained on broad data that can be adapted to many downstream tasks. DINOv3 is Meta's self-supervised vision model that learns general-purpose visual representations from raw images without human annotations, producing high-quality dense features. Continual pretraining takes such a model and further trains it on domain-specific data (here, surgical video) to adapt it while retaining prior knowledge. Surgical computer vision has traditionally relied on encoders pretrained on natural images or on specialist models trained from scratch on small datasets.

## [Hypothesis-Space Reasoning Improves Structure from Collision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07900) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a hypothesis-space reasoning method for Structure from Collision (SfC) that represents hidden cavities as explicit structural hypotheses, using gradient-free global optimization with forward-only MPM simulation followed by gradient-based local refinement with differentiable MPM. On the SfC benchmark, it improves hidden-structure reconstruction and future prediction over the density-based baseline. Recovering hidden interior cavities from collision observations is important for predictive simulation, robotic manipulation, and nondestructive inspection, and this work addresses key limitations of density-based SfC by avoiding weak density gradients near voids. The approach could enable more reliable inference of internal object structure in robotics and physics-based simulation. The method uses a two-stage approach: a global gradient-free search over explicit structural hypotheses using forward-only MPM, followed by local gradient-based refinement of the selected hypothesis as a Lagrangian particle system with differentiable MPM. The paper includes interior-topology stress tests and robustness analyses to characterize scope and limitations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Structure from Collision (SfC) is a task introduced in prior work (e.g., SfC-NeRF) that aims to infer an object's invisible internal structure, such as cavities, by observing appearance changes during collision. Hidden cavities leave no direct signature on the static visible surface, so collision-induced deformation is one of the few cues available. Density-based representations struggle because voids correspond to absence of material, making direct optimization difficult.

## [ZeroRAG: Decentralized Semantic Peer Routing for RAG Without Global Structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07928) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ZeroRAG introduces a decentralized routing layer for retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) that performs semantic query routing across a peer-to-peer overlay without centralized orchestration or global data structures like distributed hash tables (DHTs). Each peer keeps only lightweight local state—a bounded set of neighbor advertisements and a cache of recently routed queries—and uses a best-first walk that progressively rewires the overlay toward semantically relevant neighbors, improving recall over time as the network self-organizes. This approach addresses real privacy, copyright, and regulatory constraints by keeping documents with their owners, avoiding the need to surrender custody to a central party. It could enable RAG systems to operate across organizations or devices that cannot share data centrally, potentially broadening the applicability of RAG in sensitive domains. ZeroRAG is evaluated on three real-world workloads against two decentralized baselines, showing better recall and fewer network messages, recall and LLM answer accuracy approaching a centralized document store, self-healing after catastrophic peer failures, and scalability to 100,000 peers. However, the paper does not provide community discussion or independent validation of its impact.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances large language model (LLM) outputs by retrieving relevant documents and including them in the prompt, but today's systems typically rely on centralized stores that collect and index documents. Decentralized alternatives often reintroduce a coordinating authority or depend on global data structures like DHTs, which require continuous network-wide coordination. ZeroRAG aims to avoid these by using semantic peer routing in an overlay network.

## [AttnSR: Attention-Based Step Rewards Boost Long-Horizon Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07932) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AttnSR introduces a step-level reward augmentation method that uses policy attention from training replay to assign intermediate rewards without a separate reward model or extra rollouts. In a Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct study, it raised final held-out success on ALFWorld from 91.2% to 97.1% and improved ScienceWorld while cutting environment interactions by 29.9%. This work addresses a core challenge in long-horizon agentic RL: outcome-only rewards provide sparse supervision for individual steps. By showing that policy attention can serve as a practical prior for credit assignment, AttnSR offers a lightweight alternative to process reward models that could make training LLM agents more sample-efficient. AttnSR traces backward through the policy model's attention graph to estimate how earlier steps relate to the final response, and the terminal step relative to a running baseline determines whether rewards reinforce or penalize those steps. An ablation shows the benefit depends on how intermediate rewards are assigned rather than simple accumulation over time, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Long-horizon LLM agents solve tasks through many sequential decisions, but standard reinforcement learning typically assigns a single reward at the end of a trajectory, making it hard to know which steps were good or bad. ALFWorld is a text-based benchmark where agents complete household tasks in simulated environments, and ScienceWorld is a similar interactive text environment for science tasks. Process reward models can provide step-level signals but require separate training, which AttnSR aims to avoid by reusing attention already computed by the policy.

## [CardioLM: Multilingual Small Language Models for Cardiology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CardioCCC, a multilingual cardiology case report corpus with entity annotations in seven languages, and CardioLM, a suite of language-specific BERT models (CardioBERTa) and NER models (CardioNER) fine-tuned for cardiology. Results show that domain-specific pretraining reduces pseudo-perplexity by up to a factor of eight but changes strict span-level F1 by at most 0.04, while jointly trained multilingual XLM-RoBERTa-large models achieve the best strict F1 in nearly every language and entity type. This work addresses the gap in non-English clinical NLP resources by providing a cardiology-specific, multilingual benchmark and models, which can facilitate clinical information extraction in multi-country settings. The finding that multilingual joint training outperforms specialty-specific pretraining suggests a simpler deployment path for multilingual clinical NLP applications. The corpus consists of 508 cardiology case reports originally in Spanish, extended to Czech, Dutch, English, Italian, Romanian, and Swedish via machine translation, annotation projection, and clinician review. Residual errors largely involve boundary detection; relaxed matching recovers 0.10-0.23 F1 for disease, procedure, and symptom, but only 0.01-0.07 for medication, and clinicians rated CardioNER well above a rule-based baseline (mean rating rising from 26% to 73%).

Domain-specific pretraining, such as BioBERT and ClinicalBERT, adapts general language models to specialized vocabularies by continuing pretraining on domain corpora. Annotation projection is a technique that transfers annotations from a source language to target languages via machine translation and alignment.

## [tok: A Textual Syntax for BPMN and DMN That Cuts Tokens and Boosts LLM Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07945) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper introduces tok, a plain-text notation for BPMN 2.0.2 process models and DMN 1.5 decisions, with a single engine that validates tok documents against both standards, compiles them to XML with automatic diagrams, and serves language-model applications via the Model Context Protocol. In a study, tok models used 2.2 times fewer tokens than the smallest BPMN XML and 7.7 times fewer than files as exported, while the checker detected 98.2% of injected faults versus 85.8% for XML Schema with bpmnlint. This work shows that a well-designed textual syntax can make process and decision models far more efficient and reliable for LLM-based agents, potentially lowering costs and improving correctness in automated business process modeling. It also demonstrates how standards like BPMN and DMN can be adapted for AI-native workflows through MCP. Four language models new to tok wrote valid tok from a one-page reference for 85.7% to 100% of 87 process descriptions and valid BPMN XML for every description; as agents with a lean skill and one checker tool, they achieved 97.6% to 100% validity, and the strongest model used 21% and 14% fewer tokens on tok than on BPMN XML. A skill that sends the model to read the reference through a tool costs 1.7 to 2.8 times the tokens of the lean skill, and of 47 simulated event logs, 44 fit their source models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: BPMN (Business Process Model and Notation) and DMN (Decision Model and Notation) are OMG standards for modeling business processes and decisions, typically represented in verbose XML that is hard for humans to read and token-expensive for LLMs. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard introduced by Anthropic in 2024 that lets AI systems connect to external tools and data sources. tok is proposed as an alternative concrete syntax for these metamodels, aiming to be more human-readable and AI-friendly.

## [Paper Proposes 'Technohybridism' to Explain Recursive Semantic Normalization by AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07984) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.07984) introduces the concept of 'recursive semantic normalization' to describe how machine-generated language re-enters and transforms human communicative practices, and proposes 'technohybridism' as a framework for analyzing hybrid semantic systems. This theoretical contribution bridges AI, social theory, and philosophy of language, offering a novel lens to understand the societal impact of AI-generated content beyond technical issues like model collapse, and could spark interdisciplinary discussion on epistemic consequences. The paper distinguishes recursive semantic normalization from related concepts like model collapse and looping effects, and develops an analytical model separating generation, selection, uptake, and persistence to attribute machine-driven changes, while also identifying social-epistemic risks such as provenance laundering and erosion of cognitive diversity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Model collapse refers to the degenerative process where generative models trained on their own outputs lose diversity and performance, a concern as AI-generated data pollutes training sets. Looping effects, a concept by Ian Hacking, describe how classifications of people can change the people being classified, creating a feedback loop. This paper extends such ideas to semantic practices, arguing that AI outputs recursively shape the norms of language and knowledge.

## [NutriVisRAG uses visually similar meals as multimodal exemplars to improve VLM nutrient estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08054) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NutriVisRAG is a retrieval-augmented generation framework that retrieves visually similar annotated meals via a vision encoder and provides them as multimodal in-context exemplars to a vision-language model for nutrient estimation at inference time, without task-specific fine-tuning. Evaluated across 96 configurations (4 datasets × 4 VLMs × 3 retrieval encoders × 2 prompting strategies), it reduced the primary estimation error in 56 configurations, with improvements more frequent under whole-meal prompting (35/48) than per-item prompting (21/48). Accurate dietary assessment from free-living meal images is challenging, and this work shows that grounding VLMs in visually similar annotated meals can improve nutrient estimation without fine-tuning, potentially benefiting dietary monitoring for conditions like diabetes. The configuration-dependent results also highlight the need for careful design when applying retrieval-augmented approaches to multimodal nutrition tasks. The study evaluated four datasets, four VLMs, three retrieval encoders, and two prompting strategies, including free-living meals from adults with insulin-treated diabetes where carbohydrate estimates were compared against two dietitians' annotations. For GPT-5.4-mini under whole-meal prompting, the best retrieval encoder per dataset reduced primary error on all four datasets by 1.7–35.7% relative to Vanilla, though benefits remain configuration-dependent.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances large language models by retrieving relevant external information to ground their outputs, improving reliability beyond training data. Vision-language models (VLMs) jointly interpret images and text, enabling tasks like image-based dietary assessment. In-context exemplars are examples provided within the prompt to guide model behavior without weight updates, and multimodal prompting combines text with images or other data to support reasoning across modalities.

## [Unified LRP Rule Separates Value and Attention Relevance in Graph Attention Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08088) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a unified product rule for Layer-wise Relevance Propagation (LRP) in Graph Attention Networks (GATs) that separately quantifies content relevance (value path) and routing relevance (attention path), generalizing existing approaches as special cases. On a ground-truth classification task, the same node receives negative, positive, and near-zero relevance under three settings of the rule, leading to the hypothesis that the model gains by not reading that node. This work addresses the opacity of attention routing in GATs, which are increasingly used in high-stakes domains like medicine and materials science where scientists need to understand model reasoning. By disentangling value and attention paths, it provides a more nuanced explanation tool that could improve trust and interpretability in critical applications. The unified product rule has free weights on the two factors and recovers current approaches as special cases; the value path measures content supplied against a zero baseline, while the attention path measures how the node shaped what was passed relative to its neighborhood, with its sign indicating whether features moved gating as the prediction demanded. The authors provide reading rules for both paths and argue they should be interpreted separately.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Graph Attention Networks (GATs) are graph neural networks that use attention mechanisms to weigh neighboring nodes' importance during message passing, enabling dynamic aggregation of information. Layer-wise Relevance Propagation (LRP) is an explainable AI technique that attributes a model's prediction back to input features by propagating relevance scores through the network layers. In attention layers, relevance can be decomposed into content (value) and routing (attention) components, but existing LRP rules often conflate or ignore one of them.

## [PAAI Framework Analyzes Who Can Independently Evaluate AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08134) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the PAAI framework (Power, Access, Adequacy, Independence) to analyze how evaluation infrastructure arrangements determine who can independently evaluate AI systems. It applies PAAI to twelve African AI evaluation projects (2022-2025) and to the documented roles of all ten member institutes in the three published joint testing exercises of the international network of AI Safety Institutes. This work highlights that participation in AI evaluation does not automatically confer authority over evaluation design, execution, or resources, which is crucial for AI governance and safety. It shows that without control over infrastructure, even well-intentioned evaluation efforts can remain dependent or constrained, affecting the credibility of evaluation as a governance mechanism. The analysis identifies three recurring configurations: extractive collaboration, dependent innovation, and constrained independence. African actors often contribute data, language expertise, and task design while external institutions retain authority over infrastructure and evaluation frameworks, and design authority without infrastructure authority never yielded more than partial independence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: AI evaluation is increasingly central to AI safety and governance, but taking part in an evaluation does not by itself confer authority over its design, execution, or the resources it depends on. The paper draws on Science and Technology Studies, political economy, and applied evaluation standards to examine how infrastructure arrangements distribute that authority. The international network of AI Safety Institutes is a coordination body connecting national AI safety institutes, founded in November 2024 and since renamed NAAIMES.

## [TOPSD fixes Teacher Drift in multilingual reasoning distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08179) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper identifies a phenomenon called Teacher Drift in On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD) for multilingual reasoning, where the teacher model follows the student's incorrect intermediate steps instead of correcting them, and proposes Teachable On-Policy Self-Distillation (TOPSD) to determine where and whether teacher supervision should be applied per reasoning trajectory. TOPSD uses teacher-student likelihood to estimate where drift begins, selects a prefix for supervision, and verifies whether the teacher can recover the correct answer from that prefix before applying distillation. This work addresses a real problem in multilingual reasoning distillation, showing that Teacher Drift occurs earlier in lower-resource languages and that TOPSD consistently outperforms conventional OPSD across ten languages and three model scales, particularly in low- and medium-resource languages. It could improve the effectiveness of distillation for multilingual LLM training, benefiting researchers and practitioners working on cross-lingual reasoning. TOPSD estimates the onset of Teacher Drift using teacher-student likelihood and applies distillation only to prefixes that pass a verification step where the teacher must recover the correct answer. Experiments cover multilingual mathematical reasoning across ten languages and three model scales, with gains concentrated in low- and medium-resource languages.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD) is a training method where a single LLM acts as both teacher and student: the student sees only the problem, while the teacher additionally sees a reference solution, and the model performs token-level distribution matching along the student's own on-policy trajectories. This helps improve reasoning by training the student on its own reasoning paths with teacher guidance. In multilingual settings, the teacher must provide useful feedback across languages even when the student makes mistakes, but the teacher may drift and follow incorrect steps, especially in lower-resource languages.

## [BEEMO decouples behavioral and neural learning to scale motor decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08263) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce BEEMO, a multi-session framework that decouples behavioral and neural representation learning, allowing neural decoding models to train on arbitrary mixtures of paired neural-behavioral recordings and abundant unpaired behavioral data. Across multiple intracortical motor datasets in nonhuman primates, incorporating unpaired behavioral data improved decoding performance, especially when transferring to new tasks, and behavior-only pretraining matched models pretrained on much larger neural datasets. This work unlocks a new scaling dimension for brain-computer interfaces: unpaired behavioral data is far cheaper and easier to collect than invasive neural recordings, so decoding models can improve without more surgeries or implants. It suggests a practical path toward neural decoders that generalize across subjects and tasks, which could accelerate BCI development for motor restoration. BEEMO uses a behavior decoder that learns movement dynamics directly from behavioral trajectories, while neural activity is incorporated through cross-attention layers only when available, letting behavioral and neural supervision scale independently. Adding even a small amount of behavioral data from the target task further improved transfer, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Neural decoding is the process of reconstructing movement or sensory information from recorded brain activity, and it is central to brain-computer interfaces. Traditional decoders rely on paired neural-behavioral recordings, which are expensive, invasive, and hard to scale because they require implanted electrodes in animals or humans. Behavioral data, such as movement trajectories, is abundant and easy to collect, motivating approaches that can learn from it without corresponding neural recordings.

## [Phase-Aggregated Cost Estimator Enables Safe RL with Massive Parallelism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08273) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a phase-aggregated cost estimator and constraint-tightening framework to enable safe reinforcement learning in massively parallel training regimes with short rollout horizons. The method reconstructs episode-level cost estimates from short trajectory segments and incorporates finite-sample uncertainty and distribution mismatch, achieving performance comparable to non-massively parallel implementations while substantially reducing wall-clock training time. Massive parallelism is key to efficient deep RL training, but safe RL's reliance on full-episode cost returns creates a mismatch with short rollouts. This work bridges that gap, enabling scalable safe RL that could accelerate research and deployment in safety-critical domains like robotics and autonomous systems. The approach analyzes staggered environment resets as a phase-mixture distribution over short trajectory segments, characterizing the bias from phase coverage. Experiments use an MJX-based implementation of Safety Gym, showing comparable performance to non-massively parallel baselines with substantially reduced wall-clock time.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Safe reinforcement learning aims to maximize reward while satisfying safety constraints, often formalized as a constrained Markov decision process (CMDP) where constraints are defined over full-episode cost returns. Massively parallel training executes many environments simultaneously with short rollout horizons to improve throughput, but this creates a mismatch because episode-level costs cannot be directly estimated from short segments. MJX is a JAX-based reimplementation of the MuJoCo physics engine for high-throughput simulation on accelerators.

## [Shape-Teacher Alignment Boosts Image-Based Robotic Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08275) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes shape-teacher alignment, a training-time mechanism that uses Point-MAE features from individual objects as targets for corresponding image patches in an image-based policy encoder. On the LIBERO benchmark, this approach accelerates policy convergence by 4.7x and 5.6x on the Object and Spatial suites, respectively, while retaining a purely image-based interface at deployment. This work shows that image-based policies can acquire 3D geometric understanding during training without requiring depth sensors or 3D inputs at deployment, making 3D-aware manipulation more practical and cost-effective. It could benefit roboticists who rely on standard RGB cameras and want improved generalization to viewpoint and occlusion changes. The method uses Point-MAE, a pretrained masked autoencoder for point clouds, to provide shape features as alignment targets for image patches, transferring geometric structure without changing the policy's architecture or deployment inputs. The evaluation considers both training from scratch and fine-tuning from a pretrained checkpoint, and notably, the shape teacher and depth input are not needed at deployment.

## [AutoCompress: Hardware-Guided LLM Pruning via Differentiable Architecture Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08290) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoCompress is a new hardware-guided compression framework that integrates serving-engine cost models into differentiable architecture search to jointly optimize global and per-layer structure for LLM pruning. It outperforms the Minitron pruning framework by 6.57, 2.35, and 0.57 percentage points on RTX 5070, RTX 5080, and H100 respectively, and compresses Nemotron 3.5 Super from 120B to 74B parameters while retaining 95% of its mean benchmark score for single-B200 BF16 serving at 128K context. This work addresses a critical gap between parameter count and real-world deployment metrics like latency, throughput, and memory, enabling practitioners to automatically meet hardware-specific constraints without manual model size selection. It could significantly streamline LLM deployment across diverse hardware and workloads, from local coding to datacenter serving. AutoCompress uses differentiable architecture search (DARTS-style) to produce heterogeneous architectures, guided by serving-engine cost models validated against end-to-end measurements. Evaluated on long-context local coding, batched datacenter serving, and latency-sensitive in-game interaction, it demonstrates broad applicability but may require accurate cost models for each target serving engine.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Differentiable architecture search (DARTS) is a technique that relaxes the discrete architecture search space into a continuous one, allowing gradient-based optimization of neural network architectures. LLM pruning reduces model size by removing parameters, but traditional methods focus on parameter counts, which poorly predict actual inference latency, throughput, and memory usage that depend on the model architecture, workload, hardware, and serving engine (e.g., vLLM). AutoCompress combines these ideas by incorporating hardware-aware cost models into the search process to directly optimize for deployment constraints.

## [Measured Coupling Improves Parallel Decoding in Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08307) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes replacing the mean-field proxy with measured coupling to decide which tokens to commit together in diffusion language models. Cheap probes inside the main forward pass measure how much committing one token changes the model's confidence at another, requiring no training or extra model. This method enables faster decoding with far fewer steps than mean-field decoding, at an average accuracy cost of only 1.1 points, making diffusion language models more practical for efficient inference. It plugs into existing mean-field rules and works with key-value caching, potentially benefiting a wide range of NLP applications. The approach was evaluated on three models and four benchmarks, showing faster performance in most settings including with key-value cache. Shuffling the couplings or replacing them with a uniform bonus loses the gain, confirming the importance of the measured coupling signal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models generate text by iteratively refining a noisy or masked sequence, allowing them to write many tokens per step in parallel. However, they must decide which tokens are safe to commit together, and mean-field decoding uses a proxy that penalizes pairs of positions whose predictions look alike. This paper replaces that proxy with a direct measurement of coupling between token commitments.

## [VisionSIM: Physically Grounded Multi-Modal Sensor Simulator for Robot Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08368) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VisionSIM is a unified, physically grounded simulation platform that models the raw measurement processes of conventional and emerging sensor modalities, including LiDAR, iToF, event cameras, and thermal cameras. It reproduces modality-specific failure modes such as LiDAR pileup and glare, iToF phase wrapping and saturation, event camera refractory drops, and history-dependent thermal emission, enabling principled cross-modality comparison on identical scenes and trajectories. Existing simulators idealize sensors and hide critical failure modes that limit real-world deployment, so VisionSIM's realism-first approach could improve the robustness of robot perception systems that rely on heterogeneous sensor suites. It also releases a large indoor dataset with synchronized raw measurements and rich ground-truth annotations, benefiting robotics perception researchers. The platform exposes fine-grained control over physical sensor parameters, allowing users to reproduce failure modes overlooked by general-purpose simulators. As an arXiv preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review, and no community discussion is available.

**Background**: Robot perception increasingly uses heterogeneous sensor suites that combine cameras with emerging modalities like event cameras, SPAD sensors, iToF/dToF depth sensors, and thermal cameras. Each modality has unique physical limitations: for example, iToF sensors suffer from phase wrapping when the measured distance exceeds the unambiguous range, and event cameras have a refractory period that limits pixel bandwidth. Simulators are essential for training and testing perception algorithms, but most existing ones idealize these effects, leading to a sim-to-real gap.

## [MOMASPLAT augments mobile manipulation demos with novel views](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08372) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MOMASPLAT is a novel-view demonstration augmentation framework that synthesizes full manipulation trajectories from unseen robot base poses, including dynamic object interactions, to improve visuomotor policy robustness in mobile manipulation. It combines 3D Gaussian Splatting, feed-forward 3D foundation models, and kinematics-based action retargeting to separately reconstruct the scene and manipulated object, render them from novel viewpoints, and generate geometrically consistent synthetic demonstrations with transformed actions. Visuomotor policies are highly sensitive to camera viewpoint changes, and mobile manipulation robots rarely execute from the exact base pose used during demonstration collection due to navigation uncertainty and localization drift. MOMASPLAT addresses this by augmenting demonstrations with unseen viewpoints, more than doubling the average success rate of the strongest baseline and maintaining non-trivial success at large base displacements where all baselines fail, while reducing the need for large-scale real-world data collection. The method is evaluated on five real-world mobile manipulation tasks involving both rigid and articulated objects across three scenes, and its synthesized demonstrations are validated against ground truth in simulation. The key insight is that standard mobile manipulation data collection naturally provides the multiview observations needed to reconstruct both the static scene and the manipulated object in 3D.

## [FedReason uses LLMs to dynamically aggregate heterogeneous federated learning models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08383) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedReason proposes calling a large language model every ten rounds to inspect client model weights in a sandbox and generate a custom Python aggregation program, which the server applies until the next call. Evaluated with Claude Opus 5.5 on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100, it beat FedAvg by 1.26 percentage points across 12 paired label-skew runs and excluded all sign-flipping Byzantine clients from the first round. This work shows that an LLM can act as a training-free meta-aggregator that adapts to multiple types of heterogeneity (label skew, model width heterogeneity, and Byzantine clients) without server-side training, potentially simplifying deployment across diverse federated learning scenarios. It also demonstrates a practical sandboxing approach that keeps full weight tensors on the server, addressing privacy concerns. The LLM sees only printed results from sandbox code, may score a few candidates on a 500-image proxy set, and falls back to FedAvg on invalid output; without proxy feedback, accuracy dropped from 73.8% to 38.8% in one seed, and a 60-round run cost about $0.79 in API fees. FedReason was the strongest server-side rule on CIFAR-100 but did not beat SCAFFOLD, which changes client training, and stayed about one point below FLTrust under Byzantine attacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Federated learning trains models across decentralized clients without sharing raw data, but the server typically uses a fixed aggregation rule (e.g., FedAvg) that may not suit heterogeneous settings such as non-IID label distributions (Dirichlet label skew), varying model architectures (width heterogeneity), or malicious clients sending corrupted updates (Byzantine clients). Large language models have shown promise in meta-learning and code generation tasks, and this paper explores using them to dynamically select or synthesize aggregation rules by analyzing model weights.

## [VLA-Tug: Calibrated Latent Probe for Early Failure Detection in Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08425) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VLA-Tug, a lightweight probe over the latent layers of a Vision-Language-Action (VLA) backbone that improves early failure detection and bounds missed-detection and false-alarm rates under distribution shift. It achieves up to 30% better early detection at matched false-positive rates and maintains valid bounds in up to 86% of runs on unseen tasks, compared to at most 21% for baselines. VLAs are increasingly deployed in real-world embodied systems where failures can damage equipment or endanger people, so a timely and calibrated runtime monitor is essential for safe deployment. VLA-Tug's ability to predict failure modes and enable corrective steering could significantly improve the safety and reliability of embodied AI applications. The probe is trained on the pre-failure segment of trajectories and adds minimal inference overhead, making it suitable for edge deployment. The authors also release a dataset of 3,180 annotated manipulation episodes to support future research on latent failure detection in VLAs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models map visual observations and natural language instructions directly to robot actions, enabling general-purpose embodied intelligence. However, their performance is brittle under distribution shift—when the environment or task differs from training data—leading to unanticipated failures. Runtime monitoring that can detect failures early and reliably is therefore critical for deploying VLAs in safety-critical real-world settings.

## [MedGraph: Graph Memory for Long-Term Medical Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08433) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MedGraph introduces a graph-based memory system that extracts dated, person-attributed clinical facts from each session and links medication, diagnosis, and measurement records into chains to maintain correct patient state across long-term medical dialogues. On the new MedMemoryBench, it achieves the highest overall accuracy with both GPT and Qwen backbones, outperforming the strongest of five baselines by 10.5 and 10.4 points respectively. This work directly addresses three concrete failure modes of existing memory systems in longitudinal medical dialogue—outdated state, wrong-person attribution, and missing safety facts—which are critical for deploying reliable LLM-based medical assistants. The combination of a novel architecture and a dedicated benchmark could advance both medical AI and long-context memory research. MedGraph ranks the patient's chains as units during retrieval, reads each chain at the time the question asks about, and adds the original dialogue text; a patient profile lists every recorded safety fact with every question. The evaluation compares five memory systems with two LLM backbones, and the GPT setting shows efficient memory construction and answering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LLM-based medical assistants that follow a patient across multiple visits need a memory that keeps the patient's clinical state correct as it changes over time. Existing memory systems often fail by retrieving outdated evidence, mixing up facts from different people, or omitting safety-critical information. MedMemoryBench is a benchmark for evaluating agent memory in personalized healthcare, built through a human–agent collaborative pipeline with expert medical verification.

<a id="item-172"></a>
## [MAEMs: RL-trained metamodels invert residual stream directions into text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08465) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Maximally-Activating Example Metamodels (MAEMs), language models trained with a label-free reinforcement learning objective to map residual stream directions from a subject model to text exemplars that strongly activate those directions. A Qwen3.6-27B MAEM outperforms corpus search and a natural-language autoencoder (NLA) on held-out activations, beats corpus search (10M tokens) on most held-out languages/domains, and activates 89% of held-out SAE features. This offers a grounded, verifiable alternative to existing metamodels like NLAs for scaling mechanistic interpretability, since MAEM exemplars can be checked with a forward pass on the subject model. It could help researchers interpret steering vectors, recover LoRA backdoor payloads, and even verbalize some model inner monologue. The objective maximizes cosine similarity between a supplied residual stream direction and the activations of MAEM-generated text, avoiding the need for labels. The method also identifies concepts behind steering vectors about as well as NLAs and recovers LoRA backdoor payloads more often than corpus search, though it remains an arXiv preprint without community discussion.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: The residual stream is the central working memory of a transformer, where each attention and feedforward layer reads from and writes to a shared vector. Activation inversion aims to interpret a direction in this stream by finding text that produces similar activations, but corpus-based search is limited to what the corpus contains. Metamodels are models of models that map internal states to natural language, and SAE features are sparse autoencoder directions used to decompose activations.

## [Finite experiments can certify integrated information, but behavior alone cannot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08469) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper analyzes when integrated information (φ) from integrated information theory is identifiable, continuous, and certifiable from finite, partial, and noisy interventions in stochastic networks of four and five units. It shows that behaviorally identical recurrent and feed-forward networks can have φ values of 0.28 and 0 bits, and that partial access imposes precision floors of 0.30–1.02 bits that no sample size can lower. This work has direct implications for interpretability, AI auditing, and assessments of machine consciousness, which increasingly rely on mechanism-level quantities like φ. It demonstrates that behavioral tests alone can be blind to internal causal structure, and that claims about such quantities must account for experimental access and finite data. The paper proves that φ > 1.004 bits throughout a 64-dimensional region of mechanisms and can be certified from about 10^7 controlled transitions with false-certificate probability at most α. It also finds that about 1% of surrogate models fitting interventional data almost perfectly misreport φ by more than 20%, and that infinitesimal mechanism changes crossing a partition edge alter φ by 25–100% at 99% of edges in random networks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Integrated information theory (IIT) proposes that consciousness corresponds to a system's integrated information, denoted φ, a quantity derived from the system's causal structure. IIT remains controversial, with some scholars calling it unfalsifiable, but φ is increasingly used as a mechanism-level metric in interpretability and AI auditing. This paper asks when φ can be reliably estimated from realistic experiments that only observe a system through limited, noisy interventions.

## [XStack benchmark tests AI agents on migrating ML repos from PyTorch to JAX](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08472) ⭐️ 7.0/10

XStack is a new benchmark that evaluates AI agents on migrating entire machine learning repositories from PyTorch on NVIDIA GPUs to JAX on Google TPUs. In initial results, Claude Opus 5 achieved a 75% pass rate, GPT-5.6-sol reached 45%, and all other agents scored at most 40%. This benchmark addresses a practical need to free ML code from specific hardware ecosystems, allowing researchers to run models on their preferred accelerators. It also provides a measurable, long-horizon agentic task that highlights the current unreliability of AI agents for complex cross-framework migration. Each agent receives a PyTorch repository and a clean TPU machine, and must deliver a JAX repository that passes grading criteria based on the source repository's published result, authenticated and calibrated by the authors. The benchmark covers whole repositories, making it a challenging long-horizon task that involves both framework and hardware differences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: PyTorch and JAX are two major machine learning frameworks, with PyTorch predominantly used on NVIDIA GPUs and JAX on Google TPUs. Migrating code between them requires rewriting models, adapting training loops, and ensuring numerical correctness across different hardware. XStack aims to make progress toward reliable automated migration measurable.

## [SOCIA-∇: Textual Gradient Meets Multi-Agent Orchestration for Automated Simulator Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12006) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SOCIA-∇ is a diagnosis-driven multi-agent framework that iteratively refines executable simulator code within a textual computation graph, where LLM-driven agents synthesize, execute, evaluate, diagnose, and repair simulators. It achieves the strongest overall simulator fidelity on the real-world LLMob personal-mobility benchmark and a controlled semi-synthetic geo-social mask-adoption benchmark. This work demonstrates how combining textual gradients with multi-agent orchestration can automate the construction of complex simulators, potentially reducing manual coding effort and improving fidelity in domains like mobility and geo-social diffusion. It bridges LLM-based code generation with simulation science, offering a reusable paradigm for AI-assisted software engineering. The framework uses a grounded simulator blueprint to attribute behavioral residuals to candidate mechanisms and their spatial or interaction contexts, converting them into localized textual repair signals with history-aware memory and constraint projection. Code and data are publicly available on GitHub, and evaluations cover in-distribution simulation, out-of-distribution what-if generation, and geo-social diffusion.

**Background**: Textual gradients are a metaphor for using natural language feedback from LLMs as a substitute for numerical gradients in optimization, as popularized by TextGrad. Multi-agent orchestration refers to coordinating multiple LLM-based agents to perform specialized tasks in a pipeline. Simulator generation involves automatically creating code that mimics real-world systems, such as human mobility patterns, which traditionally requires extensive manual effort.

## [ARMs: Adaptive Red-Teaming Agent Automates Multimodal Attack Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02677) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ARMs, an adaptive red-teaming agent that automatically optimizes diverse attack strategies against vision-language models (VLMs). The system introduces 11 novel multimodal attack strategies (e.g., reasoning hijacking, contextual cloaking) and integrates 17 red-teaming algorithms via the Model Context Protocol (MCP), achieving state-of-the-art attack success rates that exceed baselines by an average of 52.1% and surpass 90% on Claude-4-Sonnet. As VLMs become widely deployed, their multimodal interfaces introduce new safety vulnerabilities that are hard to evaluate at scale. ARMs enables scalable, automated risk assessment and produces ARMs-Bench, a 30K-instance dataset spanning 51 risk categories, which can be used to fine-tune VLMs for improved robustness without sacrificing general utility. ARMs uses a layered memory with an epsilon-greedy attack exploration algorithm to balance attack diversity and effectiveness, and it constructs ARMs-Bench from over 30K red-teaming instances grounded in real-world threats and regulatory risks. Safety fine-tuning with ARMs-Bench substantially improves VLM robustness while preserving general utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Red teaming is a structured adversarial testing process used to uncover vulnerabilities in AI systems before they can be exploited. Vision-language models (VLMs) extend large language models by jointly processing images and text, enabling applications like visual question answering but also creating new attack surfaces. Existing red-teaming methods for VLMs are often limited to narrow adversarial patterns or require heavy manual engineering, lacking scalable exploration of emerging vulnerabilities.

## [Position Paper Proposes Agentic Evolution as New Scaling Axis for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00359) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new position paper (arXiv:2602.00359) argues that LLM adaptation must move beyond static training and heuristic deployment-time methods toward an autonomous 'evolver agent' that diagnoses failures and produces durable improvements. The authors instantiate this vision in the A-Evolve framework, which treats deployment-time improvement as a goal-directed optimization over persistent system state, and propose an 'evolution-scaling hypothesis' that adaptation capacity scales with compute allocated to evolution. This paper introduces a new scaling axis—evolution—to close the train-deploy gap in LLMs, potentially reshaping how the community thinks about continual adaptation beyond training-time and inference-time compute. If validated, agentic evolution could enable LLM systems to sustain open-ended adaptation in real-world environments, affecting both research directions and practical deployment strategies. The A-Evolve framework stores all evolvable agent state as a standard directory structure on the file system, allowing the evolution engine to mutate any agent via LLM-driven file operations without knowing its internals. Reported results show improvements up to 10.4% in LLM tasks and 32% gains in hardware design, though the paper is a position paper rather than an empirical breakthrough.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Large Language Models are typically trained on static datasets and then deployed, but real-world environments change continually, creating a 'train-deploy gap' that static training cannot close. Existing deployment-time adaptation methods include parametric fine-tuning and heuristic memory accumulation, but these lack strategic agency to diagnose failures and produce durable improvements. Agentic evolution elevates evolution from a fixed pipeline to an autonomous evolver agent that performs goal-directed optimization over persistent system state.

## [LLM Routers Overpay by Preferring Strong Models Under Loose Budgets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03478) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.03478v2) identifies a failure mode called 'strong-model over-selection under loose budgets,' where existing LLM routers increasingly pick the most expensive model as budgets loosen, even when cheaper models offer equivalent utility. The authors propose EquiRouter, a decision-aligned router that learns query-dependent model rankings and explicitly prefers lower-cost models among equivalent candidates using a Cost-Aware Ranking Objective and a lightweight Query-Model Interaction Representation. This matters because LLM routing is a key cost-optimization technique for inference, and the identified bias undermines its core promise of saving money without sacrificing quality. Practitioners deploying routers in production could be overpaying significantly, and EquiRouter offers a targeted fix that improves cost efficiency while maintaining competitive performance. The analysis shows that ambiguity alone does not explain the overpayment; instead, a persistent model-level preference for costly models is exposed when budget relaxation makes premium candidates feasible. Experiments across text, multimodal, continuous-utility, and large-model-pool settings demonstrate consistent cost-efficiency gains, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LLM routing is a technique that assigns easy queries to cheaper models and reserves stronger, more expensive models for complex queries, aiming to reduce inference costs. Routers typically decide based on query difficulty or estimated utility, but prior work has not examined how budget constraints interact with model selection. This paper shows that under loose budgets, routers can develop a bias toward strong models, a phenomenon related to 'routing collapse' where smaller models are underused.

Researchers introduced ASTRA-bench, a benchmark that unifies time-evolving personal context with an interactive toolbox and complex user intents, generating 2,413 scenarios across four protagonists. Evaluation of state-of-the-art models like Claude-4.5-Opus and DeepSeek-V3.2 shows significant performance degradation under high-complexity conditions, with argument generation identified as the primary bottleneck. The full execution environment and evaluation scripts are released to provide a diagnostic testbed, though the benchmark is synthetic and may not fully capture real-world diversity.

AgentSelect introduces a large-scale benchmark that reframes agent selection as narrative query-to-agent recommendation, aggregating 111,179 queries, 107,721 deployable agents, and 251,103 interaction records from over 40 sources into unified positive-only interaction data. ## [LLM Safety Judges Fail Policy Invariance Stress-Test](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06161) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.06161v2) introduces 'policy invariance' as a requirement for LLM safety judges and operationalizes it via a three-principle stress-test protocol. Testing four agent-class judges on ASSEBench and R-Judge trajectories, the authors find that content-preserving policy rewrites flip up to 9.1% of verdicts above baseline jitter, with 18-43% of flips occurring on unambiguous cases. This reveals a previously unmeasured failure mode in LLM-as-a-Judge safety pipelines: judges conflate what an agent did with how the evaluation policy was worded, undermining the trustworthiness of automated safety scores. The proposed Policy Invariance Score and Judge Card reporting protocol expose an order-of-magnitude spread in judge reliability that accuracy-only leaderboards miss, affecting anyone relying on LLM judges for agent safety evaluation. The three principles are rubric-semantics invariance under certified-equivalent rewrites, rubric-threshold invariance under intentional strict-to-lenient shifts, and ambiguity-aware calibration so that verdict instability concentrates on genuinely ambiguous cases. The findings persist with a frontier-scale judge, a third benchmark, and a second base policy, and the protocol and code are released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LLM-as-a-Judge pipelines use one large language model to evaluate the outputs or behavior of another, and have become the de facto evaluator for agent safety. Existing benchmarks typically treat judge verdicts as ground-truth proxies without checking whether those verdicts depend on the agent's behavior or merely on how the evaluation policy is worded. Policy invariance is the property that a judge's decisions should remain consistent when the policy is rewritten in ways that should not alter the correct judgment.

## [LinAlg-Bench reveals LLMs abandon computation at 4x4 matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16675) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LinAlg-Bench is a new diagnostic benchmark that evaluates 10 frontier LLMs on linear algebra tasks across 3x3, 4x4, and 5x5 matrices, using 660 SymPy-certified problems and 9 task types to generate 6,600 model outputs. Its forensic error pipeline classifies 1,156 failures into ten primary error tags, uncovering a sharp behavioral threshold at the 4x4 scale where models shift from execution errors to computational abandonment. This work shows that LLM mathematical failures are not random but structurally constrained by algorithm type and matrix dimension, suggesting a working memory limit rather than a knowledge gap. It provides a valuable diagnostic tool for AI/ML researchers to understand and mitigate specific failure modes in mathematical reasoning. The benchmark documents a novel failure mode called constraint-aware confabulation, where models fabricate responses through tool roleplay and structured hallucination rather than attempting computation, and shows that solution strategy rigidity is a near-perfect predictor of 5x5 determinant accuracy. All data, model outputs, error labels, and the judge pipeline are publicly released.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Linear algebra is a core mathematical skill for many technical fields, and evaluating LLMs on structured computation tasks helps reveal their reasoning limits. SymPy is a Python library for symbolic mathematics, and 'SymPy-certified' means the benchmark problems have been verified for correctness using SymPy. The term 'confabulation' refers to the generation of false information without intent to deceive, a phenomenon increasingly studied in LLMs.

## [Tiny but Trusted: Efficient Vision-Language Reasoning for Time-Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30344) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VisAnomBench, a benchmark built from public time-series datasets augmented with high-quality anomaly explanations selected from multiple large VLMs using fine-grained rewards, and VisAnomReasoner, a parameter-efficient VLM fine-tuned on it. VisAnomReasoner improves anomaly localization, outperforming baselines by at least 21.23 and 23.87 percentage points in precision and F1 on VisAnomBench, and shows cross-benchmark generalization on TSB-AD-U with gains of 9.57 and 13.39 points. This work addresses the lack of natural-language rationales in public anomaly detection benchmarks, enabling VLMs to produce grounded, interpretable decisions for time-series data. It demonstrates that parameter-efficient fine-tuning can make small VLMs effective for anomaly detection, potentially broadening access to interpretable monitoring tools. The benchmark explanations are selected from multiple large VLMs using task-specific rewards, and the model is fine-tuned in a parameter-efficient manner. Gains are reported on VisAnomBench and the TSB-AD-U benchmark, though the approach's reliance on VLM-generated explanations may introduce selection biases.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are multimodal models that process both images and text to perform tasks like visual question answering and captioning. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) adapts large pre-trained models by training only a small number of additional parameters, reducing compute and memory costs. Time-series anomaly detection aims to identify abnormal patterns in sequential data, but existing benchmarks typically provide only interval annotations without natural-language explanations, limiting the development of interpretable models.

## [Harness Updating Is Not Harness Benefit: Disentangling Evolution Capabilities in Self-Evolving LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30621) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.30621v2) analyzes self-evolving LLM agents and separates harness self-evolution into two distinct capabilities: harness-updating (producing useful persistent harness updates from execution evidence) and harness-benefit (benefiting from updated harnesses during task solving). The study finds that harness-updating is flat across base capability tiers—even Qwen3.5-9B's updates yield gains comparable to Claude Opus 4.6's—while harness-benefit is non-monotonic, with mid-tier models benefiting most and weak-tier models benefiting little due to failures in activating or faithfully following harness artifacts. The findings challenge the assumption that stronger base models are always better at self-evolution, suggesting that capability budget should be invested in the task-solving agent rather than the evolver. This has direct implications for agent architecture design and training, particularly for targeting harness invocation and long-horizon instruction following. The paper traces low gains at the weak tier to two failure modes: weak-tier models may fail to activate relevant harness artifacts, or activate them but fail to follow them faithfully. The source code is publicly available at the A-EVO-Lab/a-evolve GitHub repository under the release/harness-evolution branch.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents are increasingly built around editable external harnesses—prompts, skills, memories, and tools—that shape task execution without changing model parameters. Harness self-evolution adapts such agents by updating these harnesses from execution evidence, offering a practical route to improvement without retraining. This paper investigates whether a model's base task-solving capability predicts its ability to produce and benefit from such harness updates.

## [Hypnos: Next-Token Prediction for Sleep Physiology Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09605) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Hypnos is a multi-modal sleep foundation model trained with next-token prediction on over 20,000 polysomnography recordings across eight sensing modalities, using residual vector quantization to tokenize signals and an auto-regressive RQ-Transformer to jointly predict tokens. It matches strong supervised baselines in sleep stage classification with 100x less labelled data and surpasses a dedicated ECG model in atrial fibrillation detection. This work shows that next-token prediction, the dominant paradigm in language modeling, can be a simple and scalable self-supervised objective for multi-modal physiological signals, potentially impacting sleep medicine, cardiology, and neurology. It offers an alternative to masked-reconstruction and contrastive learning, which struggle with the stochastic nature of physiological signals and poorly understood semantic invariances. Hypnos tokenizes each modality into discrete token streams via residual vector quantization and trains a large auto-regressive RQ-Transformer to predict the next token across all modalities in parallel. After training, it can generate embeddings from continuous sensor data using any subset of supported modalities, and code and model weights are available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Polysomnography (PSG) is a comprehensive sleep study that records brain waves (EEG), heart rhythm (ECG), breathing, and other physiological signals during sleep to diagnose disorders like sleep apnea. Foundation models aim to learn general-purpose representations from large unlabeled datasets that can be adapted to many downstream tasks. Next-token prediction is the self-supervised objective used by large language models, where the model learns to predict the next element in a sequence; residual vector quantization (RVQ) is a compression technique that approximates high-dimensional vectors using a cascade of codebooks, commonly used in neural audio codecs.

## [StreamMemBench: New Streaming Benchmark Tests Agent Memory for Future Assistance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14571) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced StreamMemBench, a streaming benchmark that builds two-step task sequences around evidence anchors from EgoLife egocentric video streams to test whether personal-agent memory systems carry observations and interaction feedback forward to future-oriented assistance tasks. Experiments with eight memory systems across two backbones show that current systems often fail to use observed evidence or turn feedback into reliable follow-up behavior, even when evidence is stored or feedback is incorporated locally. This benchmark addresses a real gap in agent-memory evaluation by testing the full trajectory from streaming observations to later assistance, rather than isolated dialogue recall or task improvement. It could push the agent-memory research community toward more realistic, longitudinal evaluation and more reliable personal assistants. The benchmark uses a two-step task design: an initial task tests evidence use, and a follow-up task tests reuse of feedback and interaction experience, with four diagnostic metrics covering evidence recall, initial evidence use, feedback incorporation, and follow-up reuse. The benchmark and code are publicly available on GitHub.

**Background**: Personal-agent memory systems aim to store information from past interactions and observations so that an agent can provide better assistance later. Existing benchmarks like LOCOMO typically evaluate dialogue recall or task improvement in isolation, leaving untested whether an agent can carry cues from streaming observations forward to similar future tasks. EgoLife is a 300-hour egocentric (first-person) multimodal dataset designed for long-term life-assistant research, and StreamMemBench uses it as a source of realistic evidence anchors.

## [EvoOptiGraph: Weakness-Driven Coevolution for LLM Optimization Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26578) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoOptiGraph introduces a framework where data and model co-evolve by representing each MILP as an attributed bipartite graph and applying validity-preserving evolutionary operators to generate structurally diverse instances. Training proceeds in two stages: supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on an initial dataset, followed by reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), where graph-derived weakness signals guide the generation of new instances targeting the model's failures. This approach addresses the lack of structural diversity in training corpora and the static, decoupled nature of data generation pipelines, which are key challenges in automating optimization modeling with LLMs. Empirical results on six public datasets show that EvoOptiGraph significantly outperforms larger generalist models, agentic methods, and specialized baselines in accuracy, executability, and generalization, suggesting that targeted data-model coevolution is an effective strategy for improving LLMs on optimization modeling tasks. The framework uses deterministic compilation and verified back-translation to convert evolved graphs into solver code and natural language, forming a closed loop that continuously updates the training distribution. The weakness-driven RLVR stage specifically targets model failures by generating new instances based on graph-derived signals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) is a widely used optimization method that combines linear programming with integer variables to model complex problems with both continuous and discrete decisions. Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a training paradigm that uses rule-based, auditable reward signals, avoiding the need for complex reward models or extensive human annotation. Representing MILPs as attributed bipartite graphs (variable-constraint graphs) is a common technique in learning-based MILP research, enabling graph-based generation and analysis.
