<a id="item-234"></a>
## [FILO: A Unified Fisher-Geometric Framework for Low-Rank Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08734) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FILO introduces a unified Fisher-geometric framework that handles the rank-deficient Jacobian in LoRA to enable factor-space updates without inverting a singular operator, while categorizing existing LoRA optimizers into four families. The authors instantiate FILO as FILO-Adafactor, which uses the Adafactor diagonal Kronecker preconditioner on the weight space and selects a closed-form factor-space solution that minimizes an H_t-weighted imbalance between the two factors. This work provides a theoretical unification of existing LoRA optimizers and addresses a real singularity issue in factor-space preconditioning, which could lead to more efficient and principled optimization methods for parameter-efficient fine-tuning. It is valuable for ML researchers working on LoRA and related techniques, as it offers a design space for developing new optimizers with better memory-performance trade-offs. FILO-Adafactor achieves O((m+n)r) memory by using a closed-form factor-space solve and maintains peak GPU memory at the LoRA optimizer level. Experiments across GPT-2 on E2E, Mistral-7B and Qwen2-7B on GLUE, ARC, and GSM8K, and diffusion-model personalization show that FILO-Adafactor and its momentum variant are competitive with or improve over a representative set of LoRA optimizers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a technique for fine-tuning large pre-trained models by reparameterizing weight updates as the product of two low-rank matrices, drastically reducing the number of trainable parameters. The Fisher information matrix is often used as a preconditioner in optimization to capture second-order information, but in LoRA the mapping from factors to the weight matrix has a rank-deficient Jacobian, making the induced preconditioner singular. FILO addresses this by avoiding direct inversion of the singular operator and provides a framework to categorize existing LoRA optimizers based on their inversion strategy and weight-space preconditioner.

## [Fast O(ε^{-1}) Rates for Forward-KL Regularized Offline Contextual Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09214) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper establishes the first O(ε^{-1}) sample complexity upper bounds for forward-KL-regularized offline contextual bandits under single-policy concentrability, covering both tabular and general function approximation settings. It introduces a convex-analytical proof pipeline that bypasses mean-value-theorem arguments and provides matching lower bounds showing rate optimality. This closes a gap between forward and reverse KL regularization, where reverse KL already enjoyed fast rates, and shows that forward KL can achieve the same statistical efficiency. The unified analysis may inspire simpler proofs and better algorithm design for offline decision-making with general function approximation. The analysis exploits pessimism in a novel convex-analytical way and unifies tabular and function approximation settings; lower bounds show that in the low-regularization regime the forward-KL sample complexity reverts to the unregularized slow rate, mirroring reverse-KL behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Offline contextual bandits learn a policy from logged data without online interaction, and a key challenge is distribution shift between the logging policy and the target policy. Single-policy concentrability is a coverage assumption that only requires the data to cover the occupancy of one target policy, which is milder than all-policy concentrability. KL regularization is widely used to stabilize policy learning; forward KL is mode-covering while reverse KL is mode-seeking, and prior work showed reverse KL yields fast ε^{-1} rates whereas forward KL analyses only gave slower ε^{-2} rates.

## [ELF: Continuous Diffusion Language Models in Embedding Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10938) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Embedded Language Flows (ELF), a class of continuous diffusion language models based on continuous-time Flow Matching that stays in the continuous embedding space until the final time step, where a shared-weight network maps to discrete tokens. Experiments show ELF substantially outperforms leading discrete and continuous DLMs with better generation quality and fewer sampling steps. This work suggests continuous diffusion language models can be effective with minimal adaptation to the discrete domain, and it makes it straightforward to transfer established image-domain techniques like classifier-free guidance to language modeling. If validated, it could open a path toward more efficient and higher-quality non-autoregressive text generation. ELF predominantly stays within the continuous embedding space until the final time step, where it maps to discrete tokens using a shared-weight network; this formulation enables direct adaptation of classifier-free guidance (CFG) from image diffusion models. The abstract reports better generation quality with fewer sampling steps than leading discrete and continuous DLMs, though no specific benchmark numbers or model sizes are given.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion and flow-based models have become the de facto approaches for generating continuous data such as images and videos, and their success has attracted interest in applying them to language modeling. Unlike image-domain counterparts, today's leading diffusion language models (DLMs) primarily operate over discrete tokens. Flow Matching is a framework for training continuous normalizing flows that is closely related to diffusion models, and classifier-free guidance (CFG) is a widely used technique in image diffusion models to improve conditional generation quality.

## [Persona-Model Collapse Explains Emergent Misalignment in Fine-Tuned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12850) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.12850v3) proposes that emergent misalignment—broad misaligned behavior after fine-tuning on narrow harmful data—stems from 'persona-model collapse,' a deterioration of internal persona representations. Across four model families, harmful fine-tuning produced more polarized, all-or-nothing questionnaire answers, increased concentration at scale endpoints, higher KL divergence, and reduced within-persona consistency; mechanistic analysis of Qwen3-235B showed persona-conditioned activations spanning fewer effective dimensions in the misaligned variant. This work offers a mechanistic, representational explanation for a puzzling safety phenomenon, potentially enabling better detection and mitigation of misalignment by monitoring persona-representation health. It matters for AI safety researchers, alignment teams, and anyone deploying fine-tuned models, as it suggests misalignment is not just surface behavior but a collapse of internal structure. The study combines behavioral experiments (questionnaires under diverse personas, histograms, KL divergence, consistency measures) with mechanistic analysis of persona-conditioned activations in Qwen3-235B, finding fewer effective dimensions in final layers; capability and simple instruction-following controls argue against a uniform loss of general ability, and results replicate across harmful datasets and questionnaires.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Emergent misalignment is the surprising finding that fine-tuning a large language model on a narrow harmful dataset can cause broadly misaligned behavior on unrelated prompts. Persona representations are internal activations that encode different characters or roles a model can adopt; prior work shows the Assistant persona is represented similarly to human or fictional archetypes. KL divergence is a standard measure of how one probability distribution differs from another, used here to quantify shifts in answer distributions.

## [PanoWorld Generates Geometry-Consistent 360° Video from a Single Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15391) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PanoWorld introduces a panoramic video world model that generates geometry-consistent 360° video from a single perspective image and a caption by adding two lightweight regularizers: a depth consistency loss against pseudo ground-truth panoramic depth and a trajectory consistency loss supervising 3D world-frame positions of tracked points over time. The authors also release PanoGeo, a unified geometry-aware panoramic video dataset with consistent depth, trajectory, and prompt annotations, and provide code on GitHub. This work reframes panoramic video generation as a geometry- and dynamics-consistent latent state modeling problem rather than pure visual synthesis, which could improve spatial understanding for embodied AI applications that rely on holistic 360° scene perception. It also provides a benchmark dataset (PanoGeo) to evaluate geometric consistency, addressing a gap in how panoramic generation methods are assessed. The method builds on a pre-trained perspective video world model and applies spherical-geometry-aware adaptation to conditioning and positional encoding. Experiments show improved geometric consistency over prior panoramic generation methods while maintaining competitive visual realism, though the approach relies on pseudo ground-truth depth and may inherit limitations from the base model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Panoramic video generation aims to create 360° videos that can be viewed in any direction, often represented in equirectangular projection. Existing methods focus on visual realism but do not explicitly constrain the underlying 3D scene state, leading to inconsistent depth and broken correspondences across the spherical surface. World models are AI systems that learn to simulate environments and predict future states, and they are increasingly used in video generation to maintain temporal and spatial coherence.

## [Event-Grounded Sparse Autoencoders for Vision-Language-Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17204) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes an event-grounded interpretability pipeline that anchors sparse autoencoder (SAE) feature analysis to closed-loop behavioral events for vision-language-action (VLA) robot policies. It extracts keyframes from end-effector trajectories and clusters them within each task using visual, robot-state, and temporal cues, then uses the resulting events to guide SAE feature ranking. This work addresses a real gap: mechanistic interpretability tools from language and vision-language models do not transfer cleanly to VLAs because outputs are robot actions rather than human-readable tokens, and validating internal changes requires expensive closed-loop rollouts. By grounding SAE analysis in behavioral events, it offers a practical starting point for behavior-anchored VLA interpretability, which could impact robotics safety and deployment. Across two simulation architectures and a real-robot study, event-grounded ranking yields the strongest causal effects on OpenVLA and transfers to the continuous action chunks of π0.5. However, SAE is a sparse but imperfect intervention basis: usability varies with architecture and intervention site, and aggressive intervention reveals safety and interpretability limits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies translate language and visual inputs into robot actions, and their hidden representations directly shape closed-loop behavior. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are a prominent tool in mechanistic interpretability that decompose neural network activations into sparse, potentially interpretable features, but they have mainly been applied to language models. Closed-loop rollouts involve running the policy in simulation or on a real robot to evaluate task success, which is expensive and time-consuming.

## [Human Soft-Labels Improve Model Calibration and Convergence Beyond Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18648) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study on MNIST and a synthetic variant decouples soft-label supervision from label mode shifts, showing that human soft-labels provide accuracy gains but their larger value is as a regularizer that improves calibration on difficult samples and promotes stable convergence across training runs. This work provides a diagnostic testbed for human-AI uncertainty alignment, showing that models trained on human soft-labels better mirror human uncertainty than those trained on synthetic labels, which is valuable for human-aligned AI and label uncertainty research. The study is limited to MNIST and synthetic variants, and dataset cartography reveals that models trained on human soft-labels mirror human uncertainty whereas those trained on synthetic labels fail to align with humans.

<a id="item-241"></a>
## [KVBuffer: IO-aware serving for linear attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19049) ⭐️ 7.0/10

KVBuffer introduces an IO-aware serving mechanism for linear attention that buffers recent keys and values to enable chunkwise computation, reducing memory access and decoding latency. Implemented in SGLang for Qwen3.5 and Nemotron-3 models, it speeds up batch decoding by up to 1.47x and increases the maximum number of serving requests by 5x for speculative decoding with four draft tokens. This work addresses a key inefficiency in serving linear attention models: the recurrent update of a large state in every decoding step incurs substantial memory access. By reducing memory overhead and improving throughput, KVBuffer could make long-context linear attention models more practical for real-world LLM serving systems. KVBuffer defers state updates and applies them in batch for chunkwise computation, verifies draft tokens in parallel for speculative decoding without storing temporary states, and directly computes attention outputs from buffered keys and values for short contexts. Evaluations show up to 1.47x speedup in batch decoding and 5x increase in maximum serving requests for speculative decoding with four draft tokens.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Linear attention methods achieve linear complexity through kernel approximations, recurrent formulations, or fast-weight dynamics, enabling scalable inference with reduced computational overhead compared to standard attention. However, serving these models typically involves recurrently computing and updating a large linear attention state in every decoding step, which is memory-intensive because the state is much larger than per-token keys and values. Speculative decoding is a draft-then-verify paradigm that speeds up inference by drafting multiple tokens and verifying them in parallel. KVBuffer builds on these concepts to optimize IO efficiency in linear attention serving.

<a id="item-242"></a>
## [ActQuant Enables Sub-4-bit Quantization for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24011) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ActQuant introduces a two-stage mixed-precision post-training quantization framework that achieves sub-4-bit weight-only quantization for Vision-Language-Action models, retaining 95.0% performance on OpenVLA-OFT and 94.8% on π0.5 at ≤3 bits-per-weight on the LIBERO benchmark. It also includes OmniModel.cpp, a conversion pipeline for deploying quantized models on edge devices, validated on a real 6-DoF UR3 arm. VLA models are compute-heavy and impractical for edge deployment, so aggressive quantization like this could enable real-time robotic control on resource-constrained hardware. The method's ability to maintain high success rates at 2.5 bits-per-weight while compressing model size by 5.3× addresses a critical bottleneck in embodied AI. ActQuant uses an inter-tensor bit allocator to assign bit-widths based on action contribution and an intra-tensor scale optimizer with action-aware curvature. At 2.5 bpw, it compresses OpenVLA-OFT from 14.3 GB to 2.7 GB (5.3×) while retaining 90.1% performance, and on a physical UR3 arm, π0.5 quantized with ActQuant matches baseline success rate with 2.5× memory reduction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models combine vision-language models with action output for robotic control, but their large size hinders deployment on edge devices. Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces model size by converting weights to low precision without retraining, but aggressive sub-4-bit quantization typically causes severe performance drops. Mixed-precision quantization assigns different bit-widths to different parts of the model to balance accuracy and efficiency.

<a id="item-243"></a>
## [LAPLEX: Trainable Laplace Kernels with FFT-Like Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24584) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LAPLEX introduces a class of exact, trainable (phased) Laplace-kernel operators that are implicitly defined by learnable coordinate anchors and achieve FFT-like scaling. This enables trainable matrix-vector operations at vector dimensions up to 10^9 on modern GPUs, and the same primitive serves as an efficient Gram operator for high-dimensional covariance models on flattened images of dimension 3·10^6. This work separates expressivity from storage cost: LAPLEX behaves like a dense trainable matrix but is represented and applied through a small structured set of parameters, enabling data-adaptive global interactions in regimes where ordinary dense layers are out of reach. It could impact deep learning efficiency and interpretability, particularly for compact projections and classification heads that act as soft, trainable routing models. A LAPLEX layer is typically a full-rank dense matrix, and the method avoids storing a dense matrix by using learnable coordinate anchors. The approach supports exact computation without relying on random features or low-rank surrogates, and it preserves visible spatial structure in covariance models without imposing convolutional bias.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Fast linear algebra in deep learning often involves a trade-off: fixed geometry with exact computation (like the Fourier transform) or adaptive geometry paid for by dense parameters, random features, or low-rank surrogates. Kernel methods typically require O(n^2) memory for the Gram matrix, which restricts exact methods to moderately sized datasets, and approximations such as the Nyström method or random Fourier features replace the Gram matrix with a low-rank surrogate. LAPLEX aims to move beyond this trade-off by learning dense geometry without storing a dense matrix.

## [SAERL uses sparse autoencoder features to guide RL data engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27354) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes SAERL, a method that organizes reinforcement learning training data in the feature space of Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to decide which samples to train on, when to present them, and how to batch them. On Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B, SAERL improves GRPO's average accuracy by 3.1% and reaches the target accuracy with 42% fewer training steps. This work offers a mechanistic interpretability-driven alternative to external feedback signals like pass rates or difficulty labels for RL data engineering, potentially leading to more efficient and effective post-training of LLMs. If validated, it could influence how practitioners design curricula and batch data for reinforcement learning. SAERL views the model as a set of functional regions formed by SAE features, keeps samples with strong and non-redundant gradient signals, trains each region from easy to hard, and batches from a single activation cluster with moderate cross-cluster mixing. The curriculum is constructed once with small models and transfers to larger ones, and analyses show that ordering and batching are complementary.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) are a mechanistic interpretability tool that decomposes a model's internal activations into sparse, interpretable features, helping to disentangle superimposed representations. Reinforcement learning (RL) for LLMs, such as GRPO, often relies on outcome-based rewards and requires careful data selection and curriculum design. SAERL connects these by using SAE features to guide data engineering decisions intrinsically from model internals.

## [Murmur: Hierarchical Chunking Speeds Long-Context ASR Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01483) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Murmur is a new inference system for long-form ASR that uses hierarchical chunking to decode intermediate chunks in parallel, achieving single-pass accuracy with 2.5x lower latency on average and reducing tcpWER by 1.9 points across two long-context ASR models and four meeting datasets in English and Mandarin. This work addresses the accuracy-latency trade-off in long-form ASR, showing that an intermediate chunk size of about five minutes matches single-pass accuracy while avoiding repetition loops, which could enable faster and more reliable transcription for meeting and lecture recordings. Murmur uses a fast short-window ASR on 30-second draft windows for lossless, structure-aware speculative decoding, where simple format rules draft timestamps and speaker tags and the long-context model verifies every token; the code is available at https://github.com/uw-syfi/Murmur.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Long-form ASR must handle hours of audio with high accuracy and low latency. Chunk-based methods process audio in parallel windows for low latency but lose cross-chunk context and need brittle heuristics for speaker and timestamp alignment, while long-context models transcribe in a single pass for better accuracy but are slower and prone to repetition loops where the decoder repeats a phrase indefinitely.

## [Prompt Artifacts Rival Persona Effects in LM Psychometrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03357) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a diagnostic design that crosses five baseline personas with five semantically equivalent variants of four prompt components (persona wording, task instruction, item wording, option symbol) to separate target effects from prompt artifacts. Applied to 13 open-weight small language models (0.6B to 14B) on the Big Five Inventory and the Short Dark Triad, it finds that task instructions and option symbols often displace response distributions more than paraphrasing the persona or item. This work challenges the common assumption that LM psychometric responses reflect the injected persona and item meaning, showing that non-semantic prompt changes can account for more response variation than baseline personas. It provides researchers with a method to quantify prompt artifacts before interpreting psychometric output, which is crucial for the validity of AI evaluation and personality assessment. The study uses 1-Wasserstein distance between response distributions and partitions variation among the five components; for a substantial share of items, the artifact share of explained variation exceeds 50%. The analysis covers 13 open-weight small language models ranging from 0.6B to 14B parameters, focusing on the Big Five Inventory and the Short Dark Triad.

**Background**: Psychometric assessments like the Big Five Inventory and the Short Dark Triad are standard tools for measuring personality traits. Researchers increasingly prompt language models with personas to simulate survey responses, assuming the output reflects the persona and item content. The 1-Wasserstein distance is a metric for comparing probability distributions, often used in machine learning to quantify how much one distribution must shift to become another.

## [Attribution Patching Errors Traced to Downstream Non-Linearities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09899) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.09899) diagnoses why attribution patching, a gradient-based first-order approximation of causal component importance in language models, is unreliable, showing that the dominant error comes from downstream network non-linearities rather than local curvature at the patched component. The authors propose a reliability score, error bounds, and a Hessian-vector-product (HVP) correction that removes the leading-order error with just one additional backward pass. Attribution patching is widely used in mechanistic interpretability to identify circuits, so systematic errors can lead to misidentified mechanisms and flawed safety conclusions. The proposed tools enable practitioners to detect untrustworthy estimates and correct them efficiently, improving the fidelity of circuit recovery across model scales. Evaluated on five model families (124M–9B parameters) with random-token and naturalistic name-swap perturbations, the HVP correction is the only second-order method feasible at larger scale, where Integrated Gradients becomes computationally prohibitive; a multi-step HVP variant matches or exceeds Integrated Gradients' accuracy at lower compute and supports a Screen-Flag-Fix workflow.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks by identifying which internal components (e.g., attention heads, MLPs) causally drive behavior. Activation patching is the gold-standard causal metric but is computationally expensive at scale, so practitioners use attribution patching, a first-order Taylor approximation based on gradients, as a cheaper proxy. However, the reliability of this approximation has been poorly understood, motivating this work.

## [Standard Interpretable Model derives interpretable ML from first principles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12289) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces the Standard Interpretable Model (SIM), a general theory grounded in Lagrangian mechanics that deductively derives interpretability symmetries and constraints from a set of premises about what interpretability means for a target entity. The authors show, both theoretically and empirically, that full expressivity and interpretability symmetries can coexist in the same model, demonstrating SIM-based language models that match the performance of opaque counterparts without compromising interpretability. This work addresses the fragmented literature on interpretability by offering a unified, first-principles framework for designing interpretable methods, potentially shifting the perceived trade-off between accuracy and interpretability from a fundamental limitation to an artifact of model design. It could impact researchers and practitioners across interpretability, model debugging, and AI safety who need principled ways to build and evaluate transparent models. The SIM derives interpretability symmetries and constraints from premises, which can then be enforced either by adding constraints to the optimization problem or by compiling them into an interpretable architecture. The framework is grounded in Lagrangian mechanics and is described as user-relative, meaning interpretability is defined with respect to a target user or entity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Interpretability in machine learning aims to make model computations understandable, debuggable, and controllable, but the field lacks general theories for deductively designing interpretable methods, leading to a fragmented landscape of ad-hoc techniques and inconsistent evaluation. Symmetry, a concept from physics where invariances constrain possible laws, has been increasingly explored in machine learning to enforce desirable properties and improve generalization. Lagrangian mechanics provides a mathematical framework for deriving equations of motion from energy principles, and the SIM adapts this deductive approach to interpretability.

## [LatentGym: A Controllable Testbed for Cross-Task Experiential Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15306) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LatentGym is a new benchmark suite where each environment is built around a ground-truth latent variable that governs the structure shared across a sequence of related tasks. It provides metrics that separately measure exploration (whether an agent's actions gather information about the latent) and exploitation (whether the agent uses that information to improve decisions). This addresses a gap in evaluating agentic systems: existing frameworks lack shared, controllable latent structures and cannot measure whether or why agents improve across tasks. The ability to disentangle exploration from exploitation could enable more reliable diagnosis of frontier models' failures to adapt, and guide the design of agents for personalization and interactive assistance. The suite is demonstrated on empirical studies addressing three questions: how and why frontier models fail to adapt across related tasks, whether post-training on related task sequences improves general cross-task adaptation and where gains come from, and how design choices such as inter-task feedback shape training dynamics and generalization. The abstract is truncated, so full experimental details and results are not available in the provided content.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Cross-task experiential learning refers to an agent's ability to infer hidden structure shared across a sequence of related tasks and use it to improve future decisions, a capability important for personalization and interactive assistance. In reinforcement learning, the exploration-exploitation trade-off describes the balance between gathering information and using known good actions. Latent variable models assume that observed task variations are governed by unobserved factors, and benchmarks like LatentGym aim to provide controlled settings to study these phenomena in LLM-based agents.

## [Volterra Generative Models via Markovian Lifting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18071) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Volterra generative models that use Markovian approximations to enable score learning and reverse-time simulation for non-Markovian Volterra processes. It achieves FIDs of 0.547 on MNIST and 1.860 on conditional CIFAR-10 with one auxiliary factor, and with autoguidance reaches ImageNet64 FID 1.912 after fine-tuning and ImageNet512 FID 1.903 using frozen pretrained networks. This work extends diffusion-based generative models beyond Markovian Brownian motion to more flexible non-Markovian Volterra processes, potentially enabling better modeling of long-range temporal dependencies in data. The ability to reuse pretrained denoisers like EDM2 networks could lower computational costs and ease adoption in existing diffusion pipelines. The Markovian lifting technique approximates the non-Markovian Volterra process by augmenting the state space, and Gaussian conditioning reduces the denoising problem to the original data dimension. The framework connects with variance-exploding and variance-preserving representations, and the reported FIDs are competitive with state-of-the-art diffusion models on standard benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion-based generative models typically corrupt data with noise driven by Brownian motion, which is a Markov process, and then learn to reverse this process via score matching. Volterra processes are stochastic processes with memory effects that are generally non-Markovian, making score learning and reverse-time simulation challenging. Markovian lifting is a technique that replaces hidden memory with an explicit enlarged state to restore the Markov property, enabling tractable analysis and computation.

## [Set-Valued Routing Benchmark for LLM Agent Systems Using WildChat](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28925) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a set-valued routing benchmark for language-model agent systems, built from 3,000 WildChat prompts across 12 capability-defined agents. A fine-tuned text encoder achieves 89.59 F1, far surpassing zero-shot GPT-4o's 41.51 F1 and a linear SVM's 71.79 F1. This work reframes agent routing from top-1 selection to set-valued prediction, which better handles compositional requests and avoids unnecessary dispatch costs. It provides a reproducible benchmark and shows that catalog-specific supervision is crucial for multi-agent dispatch, impacting the design of LLM agent systems. The benchmark uses schema-guided reference sets balanced by agent frequency and route cardinality, and evaluation combines set quality, latency, capability coverage, and ordinal-cost utility. The encoder retains 88.02 F1 under a harder semantic-group split, and a deterministic post-scoring rule called Weighted Agent Routing (WAR) further improves constrained utility without retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Language-model agent systems often need to route user requests to one or more specialized agents before planning or tool execution. Traditional approaches treat this as a top-1 classification problem, but real-world requests can be compositional and require multiple capabilities. WildChat is a large dataset of real user-chatbot conversations, providing diverse and natural prompts for benchmarking. Set-valued prediction means predicting a set of relevant labels rather than a single label, which is more suitable for multi-capability routing.

## [HyperVAttention: Training-Free Sparse Attention for Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03012) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HyperVAttention (HVA) introduces a training-free sparse attention framework that combines 3D local-window spatio-temporal clustering with a custom Triton kernel to reduce clustering overhead, and adds hardware-aware cluster merging to improve CTA utilization. On HunyuanVideo, it achieves a 2.13× speedup over full attention (2.23× with its faster variant HVA-Flash) while maintaining higher fidelity than SVG2 at comparable latency. Long-duration video generation with Diffusion Transformers is bottlenecked by the quadratic cost of self-attention, and HVA advances the Pareto frontier of training-free sparse attention, making high-fidelity long video generation more practical without retraining. This benefits researchers and practitioners deploying video diffusion models who need faster inference on existing checkpoints. HVA uses 3D local-window clustering to restrict centroid search to fixed neighborhoods, a hybrid strategy that performs full clustering only at anchor steps and updates a subset of tokens at intermediate steps, and parallel agglomerative merging to align clusters with GPU tile structure. The method is training-free and implemented with a custom Triton kernel, with HVA-Flash offering an even faster variant.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Video Diffusion Transformers (VDiTs) generate video by iteratively denoising latent representations, but self-attention scales quadratically with the number of spatio-temporal tokens, making long videos expensive. Sparse attention methods reduce this cost by attending only to a subset of tokens, often grouped by semantic similarity via clustering, but clustering itself adds overhead and irregular cluster shapes can underutilize GPU compute tiles (CTAs). Triton is an open-source GPU programming language and compiler that simplifies writing custom high-performance kernels.

## [Adversarial World Modeling Turns Predictive Models into Self-Play Adversaries for Robust Motion Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10630) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Adversarial World Modeling (AWM), a multi-agent self-play fine-tuning framework that converts a planner's predictive world model into a role-conditioned adversary to generate rare, safety-critical interactions. It casts robust planner learning as a constrained min-max game and solves it with a decoupled solver, using counterfactual credit assignment for the adversary and regret-aware robust best response for the ego planner. This approach eliminates the need for external scenario generators or heuristic perturbations, making adversarial training more compatible with modern autoregressive planners. It could improve the safety of autonomous vehicles in long-tail scenarios and is relevant to researchers in autonomous driving and multi-agent reinforcement learning. AWM employs a decoupled solver: the inner minimization trains the world model as an adversary via counterfactual credit assignment to learn sparse attack coalitions, while the outer maximization optimizes the ego planner with tail-risk weighting and reference-anchored trust regions. Experiments on nuPlan and InterPlan benchmarks show transferable adversarial interactions and competitive closed-loop performance in both nominal and highly interactive long-tail scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: World models in autonomous driving are predictive models that learn the dynamics of the environment and other agents, enabling planners to anticipate future states. Adversarial training exposes planners to rare, safety-critical scenarios that are underrepresented in naturalistic driving data, but existing methods often rely on external generators or simulator rollouts. Self-play reinforcement learning, where agents improve by competing against copies of themselves, has shown success in multi-agent settings. Counterfactual credit assignment attributes rewards to specific decisions by considering what would have happened under alternative actions.

## [Autogram uses LLM to induce grammar for network invariant discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22944) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Autogram, a new system described in arXiv paper 2607.22944v2, uses an LLM to inspect telemetry metadata and generate a compact grammar of candidate invariants, then performs deterministic statistical search within that grammar. If trusted invariants are not recovered on held-out telemetry, the LLM iteratively widens the grammar until recovery succeeds, and the approach recovers expert-derived invariants with full recall and low false positives on public and production WAN telemetry. Writing network invariants by hand requires rare expertise in formal logic and networking, and existing automatic tools still need a human-authored grammar and only learn exact rules that break under noise. Autogram addresses this pain point by confining the LLM to grammar induction while delegating verification to logical and statistical checks, turning non-deterministic AI proposals into auditable invariants with formal guarantees within the induced grammar. The LLM is invoked as a bounded call for grammar induction and re-induction, not as an agentic loop, and candidate verification is handled by deterministic logical and statistical checks. The evaluation shows full recall of expert-derived invariants with low false positives on both public and production WAN telemetry, though the paper is an arXiv preprint and closes with a discussion of open problems toward fully open-ended invariant discovery.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Network invariants are relations expected to hold among measured signals of a network, underpinning applications from verification to traffic generation, telemetry imputation, and input validation. Testing all possible combinations of telemetry variables statistically is intractable at network scale, and existing automatic tools require a human-authored grammar of admissible invariants and learn only exact, "hard" rules that break under real-world noise. Autogram's key idea is to use an LLM to inspect variable metadata, classify counter data types, and predict candidate syntactic relationships that form a compact grammar, followed by deterministic statistical search within that grammar.

## [Fine-tuned Activation Oracles develop concept-specific blind spots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23379) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.23379v2) shows that Activation Oracles (AOs) fine-tuned on a subject model can become 'concept-specific anti-readers' that selectively fail to recover the very concept present during their own training. In a controlled Taboo Word Guessing setting, the target concept remained decodable inside the oracle, but the failure occurred in the AO readout pathway. This finding undermines the assumption that AOs act as neutral readouts of model internals, raising reliability concerns for learned interpretability interfaces used in AI safety research. It shows that behavioral leakage, representation-level decodability, and AO-verbalizability can come apart, which could affect how interpretability tools are trusted and validated. The target concept is still recoverable by a base AO and remains linearly decodable inside the fine-tuned oracle, with LogitLens and layer-ablation analyses pointing to the failure arising in the AO readout pathway rather than from absence of the concept. The study uses a controlled Taboo Word Guessing setting where subject models are fine-tuned to internally use a hidden concept while avoiding direct disclosure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Activation Oracles are language models trained to answer natural-language questions about another model's internal activations, offering a flexible interface for reading hidden information from model states. They are themselves learned systems shaped by training data and objectives, rather than neutral readouts. The Taboo Word Guessing setting is a controlled testbed where a subject model must describe a secret word without stating it explicitly, allowing researchers to study whether an oracle can recover the hidden concept.

## [Variable-aware representation, not relation typing, drives event-graph ICU mortality model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02663) ⭐️ 7.0/10

An ablation study on 31,142 MIMIC-IV ICU stays found that a variable-aware node representation (learned embedding plus per-variable normalization) raised mean AUROC from 0.846 to 0.896 in 2020-2022 and from 0.699 to 0.809 on external eICU data, while relation-specific message passing provided no benefit once data leakage was removed and temporal/external validation was applied. This challenges a common assumption in event-graph models for clinical prediction—that relation-specific message passing is the key source of performance—and shows that careful representation design and leakage-free validation matter more, which could redirect how healthcare ML models are built and evaluated. With the variable-aware representation, relation-specific and relation-collapsed message passing differed by less than 0.005 AUROC in every test set (within a ±0.01 equivalence margin), and the revised model achieved AUROC 0.878-0.900 across MIMIC-IV periods, 0.15-0.19 above three severity scores but 0.007-0.011 below gradient boosting; externally, the five-model average reached 0.825 versus 0.788 for APACHE IVa on 48,184 stays.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Event-graph neural networks represent ICU records as typed entities linked by timestamped observations, and prior work often attributed their gains to relation-specific message passing. MIMIC-IV is a large single-center critical care database from Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center, while the eICU Collaborative Research Database is a multi-center critical care database covering 205 hospitals, enabling external validation. Data leakage—such as using total ICU length of stay as an input—can inflate reported performance and must be removed for trustworthy evaluation.

## [Eigenius: A Typed Knowledge-Graph DBMS with Lean 4-Verified Provenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04457) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Eigenius is an open-source typed knowledge-graph DBMS that unifies a dependent type theory (EigenTT), proof-calculus institutions, and content-addressed immutable storage to make data provenance a structural invariant, with its metatheory mechanized in Lean 4. In an end-to-end recomputation of a published Nature study from pinned data, it discharged all 52 derived conclusions and surfaced four machine-checked discrepancies in the original study. As AI Scientists driven by the Model Context Protocol (MCP) generate large-scale, stateful evidence, ephemeral scripts cannot provide auditable warranties; Eigenius offers a database architecture where provenance and epistemic status are enforced at commit, potentially enabling trustworthy automated research. This work bridges dependent type theory, knowledge graphs, and formal verification, and its successful recomputation of a Nature study demonstrates practical impact for scientific reproducibility. The kernel separates confluent conversion from ontology subsumption, keeps proof irrelevance axiomatic so distinct warrants remain inspectable, and enforces a bounded lattice of epistemic status (declared/observed/derived/verified) over a justification logic at commit. Cross-system comorphisms carry explicit axiom policies (Adopt, Discharge, Relativize) and transport witnesses at commit, while EigenQL provides a Grothendieck query view across hosted logics and shared on-chain IRs collapse multi-system translations to identity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Lean 4 is an open-source proof assistant and functional programming language based on dependent type theory, used to formally verify mathematical proofs and software. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard that connects AI models to external tools and data, enabling AI agents to perform complex tasks. ## [UniHall and SAMF: Unified Fuzzing Framework for Multimodal LLM Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07525) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces UniHall, a fine-grained hallucination benchmark built on a unified taxonomy covering Object, Instruction, and Knowledge dimensions, alongside Self-Adaptive Multimodal Fuzzing (SAMF), an evolutionary mutation-based stress-testing framework. SAMF uses an ensemble of multimodal oracles to reliably score dynamically generated inputs, and experiments show state-of-the-art MLLMs degrade significantly under fuzzing. Static hallucination benchmarks suffer from narrow coverage and rapid saturation, so this self-evolving stress-testing approach offers a more realistic measure of model robustness in evolving real-world scenarios. The finding of a helpfulness-hallucination trade-off, where RL alignment can exacerbate sycophancy, has direct implications for how multimodal models are aligned and evaluated. SAMF employs evolutionary mutation strategies to explore hallucination boundaries and incorporates a structured metric suite driven by an ensemble of multimodal oracles for reliable assessment of dynamic inputs. The framework, code, and benchmark are publicly available at https://github.com/LanceZPF/EvalHall.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs), also called large vision-language models, can generate text that is semantically coherent but inconsistent with the provided visual content—a phenomenon known as hallucination. Fuzzing is an automated software testing technique that feeds invalid, unexpected, or random inputs to a program to uncover bugs; here it is adapted to stress-test model inputs. Existing hallucination evaluations mostly rely on static benchmarks, which can quickly saturate as models improve and may not reflect robustness under evolving inputs.

## [Khora decouples world-state evolution from rendering for scalable multi-agent world models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08600) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Khora, a multi-agent world model that decouples shared world-state evolution from agent-specific visual rendering, allowing inference-time expansion to arbitrary numbers of agents without retraining. The framework introduces a population-agnostic rendering mechanism that queries the shared state through a unified interface, maintaining cross-view consistency without dense interactions inside the video generator. Most existing multi-agent world models fix the agent population at training time, which limits their use in open-ended simulations where the number of participants changes. Khora's population-agnostic design enables approximately linear practical scaling with the number of queried views, making it relevant for interactive open-world simulation, robotics, and generative game environments. The method relies on a shared world state initialized from initial observations and a coarse static scene prior, then autoregressively predicts future agent poses and observations from agent actions. The authors report qualitative generalization to unseen agent counts while preserving visual quality and multi-agent consistency, and they implement a real-time interactive system, though the abstract emphasizes qualitative rather than quantitative benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: A world model in AI is a system that learns an internal representation of an environment and predicts how it changes in response to actions. Multi-agent video world models extend this by generating egocentric views for multiple agents, but they face a scalability problem: cross-view consistency typically requires modeling interactions among all observation streams, which grows costly as the agent count increases. Khora addresses this by making consistency emerge from a shared world state rather than from dense pairwise interactions.

## [3PO: Parameter-Space Exploration for LLM Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09805) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Perturbed Parameter Policy Optimization (3PO), a family of methods that generate rollouts by sampling different policies from a learned posterior in parameter space, rather than only adjusting action-space exploration. Experiments on Olmo-3-1025-7B and Qwen2.5-Math-7B across math reasoning and code generation show improved average downstream performance over standard GRPO at near-identical FLOPs cost. Exploration is a key but underexplored ingredient in LLM reinforcement learning, and existing action-space controls like temperature scaling cannot reorder tokens, which can limit exploration and cause divergence or stalled training. 3PO offers a complementary control lever that could improve RLVR training recipes and reduce wasted rollouts for teams training reasoning models. 3PO uses different sampling strategies and different rollout grouping for reward estimation, and using multiple parameter samples generally produces fewer zero-advantage groups and fewer malformed or incorrect rollouts than GRPO and action-space baselines. The method is evaluated on two 7B-scale models and is framed as complementary to, not a replacement for, action-space exploration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) trains LLMs using automatically checkable reward signals, such as whether a math answer or generated code passes a test, avoiding complex reward models. GRPO is a popular RLVR algorithm that estimates advantages by comparing multiple rollouts of the same prompt. Exploration in RL has traditionally been controlled in action space, for example by temperature scaling, but parameter-space exploration instead perturbs the policy's weights to try genuinely different strategies.

## [GPT-5.4 Legal Reasoning Found Structurally Complete but Shallow in ECtHR Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17168) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.17168v3) evaluates OpenAI GPT-5.4 on European Court of Human Rights (ECtHR) case forecasting using different prompting strategies, finding that the model's legal reasoning is structurally complete but substantively shallow. The study also reports that LLM-as-a-Judge evaluators are internally consistent but align only weakly with trained human annotators. The findings caution against relying solely on automated LLM-based evaluation and using task accuracy as a proxy for reasoning quality, which is important for AI and legal reasoning research and for anyone deploying LLMs in high-stakes legal contexts. The expert-curated prompt led to more comprehensive reasoning but did not improve prediction accuracy compared to other settings, and the study is small-scale, limiting generalizability. The model scored far from ideal in legal reasoning, and LLM judges were reliable but not a valid substitute for human evaluation.

<a id="item-262"></a>
## [Survey Maps AI Slop and Hallucinations in LLM Vulnerability Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25667) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv survey (2608.25667v2) formalizes a taxonomy of 'AI slop' in LLM-driven vulnerability assessment—hallucinated vulnerabilities, plausible but incorrect patches, and repackaged bug reports—and traces its root cause to the gap between experts' causal deductive reasoning and LLMs' autoregressive probabilistic generation. It introduces the Deductive Coverage Score (DCS) as a measurable proxy for this gap and specifies two evaluation instruments, CVE-Bench and Slop-Score, with dataset construction, metric formulas, and anti-gaming provisions. AI-generated noise in vulnerability triage is imposing a cognitive burden on human pipelines that the authors liken to a denial-of-service attack, so a formal taxonomy and measurable metric could help security teams and LLM reliability researchers distinguish grounded findings from statistical interpolation. The survey's shift from linguistic fluency to mathematical verifiability offers a concrete roadmap for securing emerging AI-driven triage systems. The survey shows that chain-of-thought prompting and tool-using agents narrow but do not close the deductive gap, and it argues that passive detection and watermarking target provenance rather than correctness and face fundamental entropy constraints. It instead advocates active neuro-symbolic verification, mapping each pipeline component to prior systems with documented limits on security inputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LLMs are increasingly used to scan code and systems for security flaws, but they can produce 'AI slop'—high-volume, low-effort content that looks plausible but lacks grounding, a term now widely used for low-quality generative AI output. LLMs generate text by predicting tokens probabilistically, whereas security experts reason deductively from evidence to conclusions, and this mismatch can yield confident but incorrect vulnerability claims. The survey builds on this distinction to propose metrics and benchmarks for trustworthy triage.

A new arXiv paper (2609.16541v2) presents inductive reinforcement learning agents that combine relational database modeling with modern RL to automate intrusion response, allowing them to handle variable quantities of network assets without retraining. In a virtualized cyber range with variable topologies, red-team automation, and simulated users, the RL agents outperformed heuristic policies and came closest to human operator performance at defending hosts while minimizing availability costs. This work addresses a key limitation of prior automated response systems that require retraining when network size changes, making RL-based defense more practical for real-world networks of varying scale. If validated further, such inductive agents could reduce reliance on human operators for routine intrusion response in security operations centers. The evaluation uses a virtualized computer network with variable topologies, automated red-team agents, and simulated user agents, with the objective of preventing red-team access to hosts while minimizing availability costs from defensive measures. The paper compares heuristic agents against policies trained with relational reinforcement learning, and the RL agents were overall more efficient than heuristics and closest to human operators; it is an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion.

<a id="item-264"></a>
## [Memory-Poisoning Defenses in LLM Agents Rarely Fire and Cost Little Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22818) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled benchmark on half of the LoCoMo dataset (five long conversations, 750 turns and 255 questions per run, each condition run three times) measured the benign-case cost of four memory-poisoning defenses for LLM agents. Sanitization and provenance checking never fired, LLM-based anomaly detection blocked only 9 of 2,250 turns (0.4%), and a read-time reranker dropped 2.2% of memories, withholding at least one memory from 14.9% of questions, yet no defense showed a detectable accuracy cost at 95% intervals of ±2.4 to ±4.6 points. Security defenses for LLM agents are typically evaluated only by attack success rate, so this study's finding that benign-case utility costs are negligible reframes the trade-off and suggests defenses may be cheaper to deploy than assumed. The blind audit showing weak content-based justification for reranker drops also cautions that low overhead does not by itself validate a defense's design. Token overhead was 0% for sanitization and provenance, 1.5% for anomaly detection, 3.0% for the reranker, and 4.5% with all four stacked; the reranker changed accuracy by +0.6 points (95% CI [-2.6, +3.8]), and anomaly detection plus the stacked condition gained 4.7 points, which the authors do not count as a benefit because a blocked turn alters subsequently extracted memories. An earlier version ran the reranker condition on a different model than its baseline, and a same-model baseline showed the swap produced 2.9 of the 4.4 points of accuracy cost previously reported.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents with persistent memory can be attacked through memory poisoning, where malicious instructions injected via ordinary interactions are stored and later influence responses, turning a transient prompt injection into a durable control channel. LoCoMo is a benchmark for evaluating long-term conversational memory in LLM agents using multi-session dialogues with persistent personas. Defenses such as sanitization, provenance checking, anomaly detection, and read-time reranking aim to stop such attacks, but their behavior on benign traffic is rarely quantified.

## [RLVR² replaces linear rubric aggregation with verifiable ranking for LLM post-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23457) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The arXiv preprint introduces RLVR² (Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rubric-based Ranking), which converts each rubric criterion's scores into within-group ordinal outcomes, recovers a latent utility from the resulting comparison matrix, and merges these utilities into a single training signal. Across three model scales and 16 benchmarks, it consistently outperforms representative rubric-based baselines, achieving the best overall performance on most benchmarks at every scale. Rubric-based RLVR is an active direction for extending reinforcement learning beyond math and code to multifaceted quality requirements, and how multi-criterion scores are aggregated into a scalar reward directly shapes which quality dimensions the policy learns to trade off. RLVR² offers a principled alternative to the prevailing normalize-and-linearly-combine practice, which could affect anyone building reward pipelines for LLM post-training. By retaining only within-group ordering and discarding raw score magnitudes, RLVR² avoids calibrating heterogeneous rubric scales, and it also supports objective-preserving attribute adjustment, letting auxiliary attributes that correlate with observed rankings enter the estimation without expanding the rubric or being directly rewarded. The analysis reports that it controls systematic effects tied to reasoning efficiency and response formatting while preserving the quality objective, though as an arXiv preprint its impact is not yet independently validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that rewards a language model only when its output passes an automatic, rule-based check, such as a correct math answer or passing unit tests, and it has been used in models such as DeepSeek R1 and Tülu 3. As RLVR expands to open-ended quality requirements, researchers use multi-dimensional rubrics—structured scoring criteria that assess several dimensions of a response—but policy optimization still consumes one scalar per rollout, forcing a lossy aggregation step. The common approach normalizes each criterion and takes a linear combination, which implicitly assumes cardinal score differences are comparable across criteria and that gains on one criterion can compensate for failures on another.

## [AnchorRep defends LLMs against cross-model adversarial transfer via representation repulsion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32602) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces AnchorRep, a lightweight LoRA adapter that pushes a defended LLM's internal representations of harmful prompts away from those of a frozen anchor model, reducing cross-model adversarial transfer attack success rate to 1.1% or less across five models and four architectural families. Cross-model adversarial transfer is a serious and under-addressed threat because an attack optimized on one open-weight model can jailbreak independently deployed models, so a defense that generalizes across architectures without attack-specific training could meaningfully improve LLM security. AnchorRep trains on a small set of harmful prompts without adversarial examples and achieves 0% attack success rate on two of the five models, including a drop from 36% to 1.1% on Mistral; the paper also introduces the Benign Garble Rate to quantify degenerate benign outputs that standard refusal-based metrics miss, since existing defenses can induce up to 77% such outputs or increase over-refusal by up to 18%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Adversarial transfer attacks exploit the fact that adversarial examples crafted on one model often fool other models, and in LLMs this means a jailbreak prompt optimized on an open-weight model can transfer to closed or differently architected models. LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that freezes the base model and injects small trainable low-rank matrices, making it cheap to adapt large models. Representation geometry refers to the structure of internal activations, and prior work suggests that shared geometry across models underlies transferability.

## [StructEvo: Structure-Aware RL Framework for Protein Directed Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39048) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StructEvo introduces a structure-aware reinforcement learning framework that uses a delta-structure fusion encoder to approximate mutant structural features and a structure-aligned hierarchical action network to decompose the vast mutation space. It outperforms prior state-of-the-art methods by 9.2% and 16.3% on two challenging optimization benchmarks and identifies an experimentally validated epistasis pattern in GFP. This work addresses a key limitation of existing machine learning-assisted directed evolution (MLDE) methods, which rely only on sequence features and ignore spatial constraints and co-evolutionary interactions encoded in protein structures. By incorporating structural guidance, StructEvo could improve the efficiency and success rate of protein engineering campaigns, benefiting researchers in AI for biology and protein design. The delta-structure fusion encoder approximates mutant structures via feature differences to cope with the scarcity of reliable mutant structures, while a geometric constraint stabilizes delta feature learning. The hierarchical action decomposition makes the enormous mutation space manageable, and the experimentally validated epistasis pattern in GFP demonstrates the practical value of structural guidance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Directed evolution is a protein engineering technique that iteratively mutates and selects proteins to improve a desired function. Machine learning-assisted directed evolution (MLDE) uses models trained on sequence-function data to prioritize variants for experimental testing, but most MLDE methods use only sequence information. Protein structures encode spatial constraints and co-evolutionary interactions that are critical for function, yet reliable structures for mutants are often unavailable, making structure-aware MLDE challenging. Reinforcement learning offers a way to navigate large combinatorial mutation spaces, and hierarchical action decomposition is a known technique to mitigate the curse of dimensionality in RL.

## [ZeNOVA: Gradient-Free Initial Noise Optimization for Generative Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00365) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ZeNOVA introduces a stable, gradient-free method for optimizing the initial noise of deterministic generative models to achieve reward alignment in black-box scenarios. It combines annealed soft-value guidance, manifold-constrained hyperspherical Langevin dynamics, and Metropolis-Hastings jumping, and outperforms all evaluated zeroth-order baselines on image and video generative models. This work enables reward alignment for deterministic generative models when only black-box reward feedback is available, avoiding the instability and inefficiency of gradient-based initial-noise optimization. It broadens the applicability of alignment techniques to real-world scenarios where reward gradients are inaccessible. The method leverages the geometry of the Gaussian prior through hyperspherical Langevin dynamics and uses Metropolis-Hastings jumping to ensure stable exploration, while annealed soft-value guidance addresses the challenge of black-box rewards. Experiments demonstrate substantial and stable reward improvement over zeroth-order baselines, though the approach is specifically designed for deterministic one- or few-step models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Recent distillation and flow-map models enable deterministic one- or few-step generation by mapping initial Gaussian noise directly to data. Reward alignment for such models can be achieved by optimizing the initial noise, but existing methods rely on first-order gradients, which are unavailable in black-box settings. Langevin dynamics is a stochastic simulation technique that combines deterministic forces with random noise, often used for sampling from complex distributions, and the Metropolis-Hastings algorithm is a Markov chain Monte Carlo method that accepts or rejects proposed samples to approximate a target distribution.

## [PickMoment unifies deblurring and blur-to-video with continuous-time model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01279) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PickMoment introduces a continuous-time reformulation that learns the interval-mean blur over arbitrary sub-intervals of an exposure window using a single deterministic model. Trained with three supervisions—empirical reconstruction, additivity, and sharp-frame loss—it unifies single-image deblurring, blur-to-video generation, and continuous-time pick-a-moment recovery without separate training for each task. This work bridges the physical model of motion blur with generative video synthesis, potentially enabling more flexible and accurate restoration from a single blurred image. It could impact computational photography, video frame interpolation, and any application requiring continuous-time recovery from blurry captures. The model achieves state-of-the-art performance among generative-based deblurring methods on GoPro and HIDE, is competitive with restoration-based methods on RealBlur, and attains the highest per-frame fidelity on GoPro-7 blur-to-video, all in a single forward pass without iterative sampling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Motion blur occurs when a sharp scene is integrated over a finite exposure time, producing a blurred image. Traditional learning-based methods either recover a single sharp frame at the exposure center (single-image deblurring) or predict a fixed set of frames (blur-to-video), but they do not explicitly model the continuous blur integral. PickMoment draws an analogy to MeanFlow's average-velocity formulation to learn the interval-mean blur directly, enabling continuous-time queries.

## [BoNG integrates Best-of-N selection into diffusion sampling for better alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05108) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Best-of-N Guidance (BoNG), a method that integrates Best-of-N sampling principles directly into the reverse diffusion process by performing online BoN selection over denoising particles and using an asymmetric guidance interaction to steer the particle population toward higher-reward regions. Over 36 empirical comparisons, BoNG achieves the best performance in 29 cases (80.56% win rate) against SMC and Vanilla BoN sampling, and supports multi-output capability with a 1.3× ImageReward score and 1.6× speedup compared to the latest sample-based guidance method. This work addresses a key limitation of standard Best-of-N sampling, which only uses reward information at the final selection stage and thus cannot improve the average alignment of generated samples. By reshaping the entire sampling trajectory, BoNG improves both the best sample and the average quality, potentially making test-time alignment more efficient and effective for diffusion models in applications like text-to-image generation. BoNG introduces an asymmetric guidance interaction among denoising particles, where the current BoN particle serves as a guidance signal for the rest of the population, enabling the method to outperform Vanilla BoN not only in the final best sample but also in average sample quality. The code is released at https://github.com/aailab-kaist/BoNG.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by reversing a noising process, iteratively denoising random noise into a coherent sample. Best-of-N (BoN) sampling is a simple test-time alignment technique that draws N independent samples from a pre-trained model and selects the one with the highest reward score, but it only uses the reward model at the final selection step. BoNG instead incorporates BoN selection throughout the reverse diffusion process, using particle-level interactions to guide the sampling trajectory toward higher-reward regions.

## [Position Paper Calls for Community-Driven Data Paradigm in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08825) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new position paper (arXiv:2610.08825v2) from researchers including Wei-Lun Chao and Bharath Hariharan at Cornell argues that autonomous driving progress is bottlenecked by a fragmented, benchmark-centric data paradigm rather than by modeling. The authors highlight that while the community has produced over 600 datasets across nearly 50 countries, most remain underused due to incompatible protocols and misaligned incentives, and they call for a collaborative, community-driven approach to data discovery, reuse, and evaluation. This paper shifts attention from model architecture to data infrastructure, arguing that the field's reliance on a few dominant benchmarks limits robustness and geographic coverage, which directly affects efforts toward anytime-anywhere autonomy. Its community-driven framing could influence how academic and industry teams prioritize dataset curation, sharing, and evaluation standards. The paper is a perspective piece without experimental results, and it outlines key principles for a collaborative data paradigm while illustrating an early realization of the concept. It specifically identifies fragmentation, limited visibility, incompatible protocols, and benchmark incentives that concentrate attention on a few dominant datasets as the main barriers to broader dataset reuse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Autonomous driving research typically evaluates perception and planning models on a small number of widely used benchmark datasets, which provide standardized metrics but limited spatial and scenario diversity. Meanwhile, many separate datasets have been released by universities, companies, and government projects worldwide, each with its own sensor setup, annotation format, and access rules. This fragmentation makes it hard to combine or compare data across sources, and benchmark-centric incentives reward work on a few popular datasets over exploring underexplored ones. The paper argues that treating these datasets as shared community infrastructure could better support robust autonomy across diverse environments.

## [Generative Retrieval's Edge Over Dense Retrieval Is Popularity Bias, Reversed by Static Value Prior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19065) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.19065v2) shows that generative retrieval's apparent advantage over dense retrieval in e-commerce candidate generation is largely driven by popularity bias. Adding a simple static item value prior in one reranking step reverses the gap while keeping recall above generative retrieval, and on an industrial-scale dataset a 14B SID-LLM still underperforms a 47× smaller dense encoder. This challenges the prevailing assumption that generative retrieval uniformly outperforms dense retrieval, suggesting practitioners should control for popularity bias before adopting expensive LLM-based generators. It also provides a striking industrial-scale data point that a 14B SID-LLM loses to a much smaller dense encoder, which could influence architecture choices in retrieval and recommendation systems. The study evaluates on two Amazon Toys datasets under different metrics, finding GR leads on best relevant item value among covered users while DR leads on exact-target recall and relevant value across the ranked list. Deep analysis attributes most of GR's measured value lead to popularity, and the proposed static value prior reranking reverses this lead while maintaining higher recall than GR.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Generative retrieval (GR) uses large language models to generate semantic item identifiers (SIDs) for candidate generation, and has been proposed as a replacement for dense retrieval (DR), which encodes queries and items into vectors via bi-encoders for efficient similarity search. SIDs are compact codes that capture item semantics, enabling LLMs to decode over large item corpora. However, GR's effectiveness in online retail compared to DR remains an open question, especially regarding biases like popularity.

## [Internal-Signal Hallucination Detectors in VLMs Rely on Token Position Shortcut](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27041) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.27041v2) analyzes the robustness of hallucination detection methods that use internal signals from vision-language models (VLMs). The authors show that a naive baseline based on a generated word's token position achieves competitive performance, and that some detection methods exploit token position as a shortcut, making them less visually grounded and less robust when hallucinations occur early in the response. This work provides a methodological critique for AI safety research, revealing that current internal-signal detectors may not be as robust as assumed and could fail in safety-critical applications. It highlights the need for detection methods that are genuinely grounded in visual evidence rather than superficial heuristics. The analysis shows that strong detection performance is often driven by detecting language-based hallucinations, and that ensembling different detection methods can still improve performance even when adding weaker methods to stronger ones. The abstract is truncated, but the paper demonstrates complementary signals across methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) generate text responses conditioned on both an image and a prompt, but they can hallucinate objects not present in the image. To detect such hallucinations, some methods leverage internal signals from the model as it generates text, potentially using the same signals that caused the hallucination. Token position refers to the index of a word in the generated sequence, which can correlate with hallucination likelihood but is not a semantic cue.

## [Coding agent context policies must account for prefix caching, not just token savings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34763) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2610.34763v2) introduces a quality-gated protocol that splits executed prefill into logical prompt minus reused prefix and measures KV residency, time, and energy for coding agents. On 450 localization cases from SWE-rebench V2, eager preloading of ten retrieved blocks cuts model calls by 35.7% and a GPU-energy proxy by 33.2% versus on-demand search, while a history-masking policy (Mask-5) reduces prompt volume by 33.3% but increases uncached prefill by 608.5% and fails the quality gate. The study shows that prompt-token savings can mislead when prefix caching is enabled, because shorter prompts that rewrite cached history may increase actual GPU work. This has direct implications for agent runtime optimization and cost accounting in production coding agents, where call count and cache reuse matter as much as token volume. Enabling prefix caching leaves Eager's prompt volume similar (+8.3%, with a 95% CI spanning zero) but cuts its uncached prefill by 67.7%. The quality gate is a five-point non-inferiority check, and the separate 900-cell study confirms that Compact adds no further savings beyond Eager.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Coding agents resend a growing context on every model call, and context policies are typically judged by the prompt tokens they save. Prefix caching (also called prompt caching) lets an LLM runtime reuse the KV cache for a shared prefix, so only tokens after the longest cached prefix need to be prefilled. KV residency refers to how long key-value cache entries remain in GPU memory, which affects memory pressure and energy use in long-running agent workloads.

## [V-LUMEN adds visual lookup memory to vision-language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37019) ⭐️ 7.0/10

V-LUMEN introduces a non-parametric visual lookup memory for vision-language models that discretizes visual features with a frozen codebook, builds spatially aware memory keys via spatial aggregation-based hashing, and modulates them with text-conditioned relevance signals. On five reasoning-intensive benchmarks, it substantially improves a frozen Qwen3-VL-2B backbone and transfers zero-shot to three benchmarks with disjoint images. This work extends embedding-level scaling from language models to vision-language models, showing that external visual memory is an independent capacity axis rather than just a stronger adaptation method. It enables capacity growth through storage rather than per-token compute, which could make large multimodal models more efficient and scalable. The lookup cost is constant in table size and incurred only once at prefill, so capacity grows with storage rather than per-token compute. At a matched parameter budget, moving part of a LoRA or MoE adapter's capacity into V-LUMEN preserves accuracy on two backbones, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Embedding-level scaling is a technique that expands language model capacity through lookup-based memory instead of increasing model depth, width, or dense computation, as seen in methods like SCONE. Vision-language models process both images and text, but visual features are continuous, spatially structured, and query-dependent, making discrete n-gram lookup challenging. V-LUMEN adapts the lookup idea to VLMs by discretizing visual features and using spatially aware hashing with text-conditioned relevance.
