## [JADE: VLM as Joint Denoiser for Robotic Action Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01215) ⭐️ 7.0/10

JADE introduces a joint action denoiser where a pretrained vision-language model (VLM) participates in every diffusion denoising step, reading the current noisy action along with visual observations and language instructions and guiding the action expert via gated cross-attention. The VLM is also supervised to recover clean action latents from noisy ones, coupling perception with control, and achieves a 12.67% average success rate on RoboDojo and a 20.8 percentage point improvement over π0.5 across four real-world bimanual tasks. This addresses a key limitation of current diffusion-based vision-language-action (VLA) models, where the VLM only provides static conditioning and cannot adapt its guidance as actions are refined, especially in out-of-distribution settings. By letting pretrained perception and reasoning guide every denoising step, JADE could improve robustness and generalization of robotic manipulation policies without requiring large-scale VLA pretraining. Joint denoising enables JADE to generate actions with fewer sampling steps, offsetting the extra cost of VLM participation, and an adaptive step-size schedule further balances task performance and inference latency. On RoboDojo, JADE achieves the highest average success rate among evaluated π0.5-based methods and is competitive with recent VLA and world-action model (WAM) foundation models pretrained at scale, while also improving performance on RoboTwin 2.0.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate data by starting from random noise and iteratively removing noise over many denoising steps, with each step typically handled by a learned network. Vision-language-action (VLA) models, such as Google DeepMind's RT-2, combine pretrained vision-language models with action outputs to let robots follow language instructions from visual input. In most diffusion-based VLA designs, the VLM is used only once to produce a fixed conditioning signal, while a separate action expert performs the denoising, which limits how much the VLM's reasoning can influence the generated action trajectory.

## [OpenNWM: Generalist Navigation World Model with Latent Action Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01844) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OpenNWM introduces a generalist navigation world model that learns latent actions from action-free videos via visual prediction pretraining, then aligns them with physical motions through action-conditioned post-training. The work is supported by NavAnywhere, a new large-scale action-free navigation video dataset comprising 17.5 million visual observations from 15 heterogeneous sources covering ground robots, humans, and drones across five scene types. This approach addresses a key limitation in navigation world models: the scarcity of action-labeled data for generalization across real-world environments. By leveraging diverse action-free videos, OpenNWM could significantly improve the scalability and robustness of embodied AI systems for open-world navigation. The dataset includes 17.5 million observations from 15 sources, capturing diverse viewpoints and motion patterns across residential areas, public and commercial spaces, urban settings, parks, and natural landscapes. Experiments show improved visual prediction on in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks, as well as better navigation performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Navigation world models are controllable video generation models that predict future visual observations based on past observations and navigation actions, enabling planning and control for robots. Latent action pretraining is a technique that learns action representations from videos without action labels, which can then be fine-tuned with a small amount of action-labeled data to map to real actions. This work combines these ideas to improve generalization in open-world navigation.

## [Sonar-MoE updates expert gradients during backward pass to cut memory and time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03577) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Sonar-MoE introduces an optimizer-in-backward approach for Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models, updating each expert tensor during the backward pass and freeing its gradient immediately. When fine-tuning OLMoE-1B-7B and Granite-3.1-3B-A800M in bf16, it reduces peak GPU memory by 19-22% and step time by 17-23% compared to standard training with global-norm clipping, while producing bitwise-identical updates. MoE models are becoming a dominant architecture for scaling large language models, but their expert gradients consume huge GPU memory that grows with model size. Sonar-MoE offers a practical, drop-in optimization that significantly reduces memory and time without changing training semantics, making MoE training more accessible and scalable. The method updates whole batched expert tensors rather than individual experts, avoiding a 20-72% slowdown while using the same memory. It matches optimizer-in-backward over all parameters in memory and speed while still clipping dense parameters, and held-out loss stays within 0.003 of all baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is a neural network architecture where only a subset of parameters (experts) is activated per input token, enabling large total parameter counts with low inference cost. During standard training, gradients for all experts are kept in GPU memory until the optimizer step, which becomes a major memory bottleneck as the number and size of experts grow. Optimizer-in-backward is a technique that applies parameter updates immediately after gradient computation in the backward pass, freeing gradients early; Sonar-MoE extends this to batched MoE expert tensors.

## [P²-MOPD: Accelerating Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation via Parameter Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03953) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes P²-MOPD, which jointly optimizes the mixing coefficients of expert parameters and the student weights under a single on-policy distillation objective, treating the linear combination of student and expert parameters as one differentiable model. Across four domains at 1.7B and 4B scales, it surpasses MOPD's final average recovery in half the training steps, with the 1.7B model reaching 94.7% average recovery versus 80.9% for MOPD. This work bridges task arithmetic model merging and on-policy distillation, offering a more efficient way to consolidate multiple domain-specialized teachers into a single student. It could reduce the compute cost of multi-teacher post-training and benefit researchers working on model merging, distillation, and LLM training pipelines. Unlike task arithmetic, which stays within the span of frozen student and expert parameters, P²-MOPD keeps the student trainable so experts act as parameter priors without confining the model to that span. The method is evaluated at 1.7B and 4B scales across four domains, showing faster and more balanced recovery than standard MOPD from the same initialization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD) trains a student model by supervising its own generated trajectories with multiple domain-specialized teacher models, consolidating their capabilities into one model. Task arithmetic is a training-free model merging technique that combines parameters of fine-tuned models via linear arithmetic to obtain a multi-task model. P²-MOPD combines these ideas by making the linear combination differentiable and jointly optimizing mixing coefficients with the student during on-policy distillation.

## [RoboTryOn: Virtual Try-On Turns Human Videos into Robot Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05163) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RoboTryOn reframes human-to-robot demonstration translation as a virtual try-on problem: it keeps the demonstrated motion and swaps the human for a target robot specified by a reference image, while preserving the task, objects, and background. The method translates selected keyframes autoregressively to guide video synthesis with motion conditioned on the human hand skeleton, and uses a depth-aware inverse dynamics model (IDM) to infer action labels for policy learning. This approach addresses the bottleneck of costly teleoperated demonstrations for high-DoF robots by leveraging scalable egocentric human videos, improving average success on novel tasks from 2% to 60% without any real robot demonstrations. It matches teleoperation-trained policies at roughly one-third of the collection cost, potentially accelerating robot foundation model adaptation across embodiments. Experiments across multiple humanoid embodiments show that policies trained with RoboTryOn synthetic data achieve 60% average success on novel tasks, including challenging bimanual manipulation, compared to 2% without such data. The approach relies on keyframe translation and a depth-aware IDM, and the paper is an arXiv preprint (2611.05163v3) without community discussion yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Robot foundation models often require teleoperated demonstrations to adapt to new tasks, which are expensive and slow to collect. Egocentric human videos offer a scalable alternative, but directly overlaying rendered robots or retargeting human poses leaves visual or action gaps, especially for high-DoF embodiments like humanoid hands. Virtual try-on is a computer vision technique that swaps garments on a person while preserving body and background; RoboTryOn adapts this idea to swap the human for a robot while preserving motion and scene.

## [LIBRA builds executable tool libraries for LLM agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05445) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LIBRA introduces a framework that converts a flat tool catalog into an executable tool library by having an LLM propose per-tool contracts (candidate invocations, reusable outputs, state changes, and success conditions) and then validating these compositions in a sandbox. On AppWorld, BFCL, and SciToolEval, LIBRA improves one-shot Recall and NDCG by 7.9 and 3.7 percentage points on average over the same dense retriever, and within ToolReAGt it yields gains of 4.5 and 2.9 points after ten retrieval iterations. Most tool retrievers rank each tool independently by request similarity, which for multi-tool tasks recovers the goal-matching tool but omits prerequisite tools required by the workflow. LIBRA addresses this gap by capturing runtime-validated tool relations, potentially improving the reliability of LLM agents that must chain multiple tools to complete complex tasks. The library is constructed before user queries and requires no dependency annotations, tool-use trajectories, or task-specific training, and it supports both one-shot and agentic retrieval. The approach relies on an LLM to propose tool contracts and on sandbox testing to retain only relations supported at runtime, which may introduce overhead and depend on the quality of the LLM's proposals.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: LLM agents increasingly retrieve tools from large catalogs to perform tasks, but standard retrievers treat each tool independently and rank by similarity to the user request. In multi-tool workflows, this often misses prerequisite tools that must be called first, causing failures. Benchmarks like AppWorld, BFCL, and SciToolEval are used to evaluate tool retrieval and tool-use capabilities of LLM agents.

## [Whiel: A Neurosymbolic Platform for Certified Relational Program Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05612) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Whiel is a new Lean-certified neurosymbolic system for verifying imperative relational programs, combining LLM-based loop invariant discovery with verification conditions checked by the Vampire theorem prover. It can encode containment and equivalence problems for Datalog and its extensions, as well as optimized evaluation algorithms and first-order integrity constraints. This work bridges formal methods and AI by using LLMs to propose invariants while relying on symbolic provers and Lean for trust, potentially making verification of undecidable relational query languages more practical. It could impact data integration, exchange, and sharing where complex query containment and equivalence problems arise. Whiel uses prophecy variables and leveled invariants to support inductive Hoare-style reasoning, feeds counterexamples back into the neurosymbolic pipeline, and translates proposed solutions into Lean proof certificates. The system is evaluated on a curated benchmark suite, though the abstract notes the problems are typically undecidable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Datalog is a recursive query language for databases, and checking containment or equivalence of Datalog queries is undecidable in general. Loop invariants are assertions that hold before and after each loop iteration, essential for deductive verification. Vampire is a leading first-order theorem prover, and Lean is an interactive theorem prover used to produce machine-checkable certificates.

<details><summary>References</summary>

## [Text-Only LLM Training Enables Zero-Shot Video Temporal Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06149) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers trained a text-only LLM (Qwen3-8B with LoRA) on a 134K-example corpus of textual video descriptions, without any paired video data, and evaluated it on Charades-STA and QV-Highlights. Their proposed Nearest Entity Replacement (NER) method achieved 14.45 mtIoU on Charades-STA and 20.72 on QV-Highlights, outperforming the strongest modality-gap baseline by 5.00 and 1.71 points respectively. This work shows that video temporal localization can be partially learned from text alone, potentially reducing the costly need for precise temporal annotations and paired video-text data in multimodal training. It represents a step toward training multimodal models without paired annotations, which could lower barriers for video understanding research. The study found a large modality gap: replacing text with frame embeddings at inference yielded only 4.99 mtIoU on Charades-STA and 11.12 on QV-Highlights. LLM2CLIP's text encoder, supporting longer inputs, represented detailed descriptions better than CLIP (61.54 vs 53.86 mtIoU on Temporal-TextVid) and transferred better to video, but the authors note that more reliable grounding will likely require encoders with better-aligned text and visual representations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Video temporal localization (or temporal grounding) is the task of identifying the start and end times of a specific moment in a video given a natural language query. Traditionally, video-LLMs are trained on paired video-text data with precise temporal annotations, which are expensive to collect. Zero-shot learning refers to performing a task without training on data from that task's domain. CLIP is a model that learns joint text-image representations, and LLM2CLIP enhances CLIP's text encoder using a large language model to handle longer and more complex captions.

<a id="item-284"></a>
## [EvoWorld: New Benchmark for Out-of-Sight Causal Evolution in Video Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06748) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoWorld introduces a new problem called Out-of-Sight Causal Evolution, which requires generative video models to reason about the consequences of events and render causally plausible outcomes even when they occur outside the current view. The authors propose a baseline framework where a vision-language model acts as a 'world brain' that maintains world state and reasons about transitions, while a segment-autoregressive video renderer visualizes them, along with EvoWorld-Data and EvoWorld-Bench for training and evaluation. This work shifts the focus of video generation from visual fidelity to behavioral consistency and causal reasoning, which is crucial for using video models as reliable world simulators in robotics, autonomous driving, and interactive entertainment. It provides a measurable benchmark and a reference solution that could spur further research into reasoning-guided video generation. The framework pairs a vision-language model with a segment-autoregressive video renderer, and the benchmark evaluates event realization, causal reactions, and revisit consistency. Experiments show substantial improvements over recent models in event-conditioned causal evolution while preserving visual quality, though the approach is a preprint and may have limitations in scalability and generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Generative video models, such as diffusion-based models, have been increasingly explored as world simulators that can predict future frames. However, they often lack explicit mechanisms for maintaining a consistent world state and reasoning about causal consequences, leading to outputs that are visually plausible but behaviorally static or inconsistent. Out-of-sight causal evolution refers to the ability to infer and render events and their effects that occur beyond the current field of view, which is essential for coherent long-horizon simulation.

## [KDASO: Dual-Driven Automated Skill Optimization for LLM Liability Adjudication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06972) ⭐️ 6.0/10

KDASO proposes a knowledge-data dual-driven method that continuously evolves a skill library for LLM-based liability adjudication through neighborhood-conditioned induction from recent adjudication data, execution-aligned refinement based on LLM trajectory analysis, and context-budgeted routing for scalable skill selection. Experiments across ride-hailing adjudication, legal liability determination, and logical reasoning judgment show KDASO significantly outperforms general-purpose and domain-specific baselines, with an 8.96% improvement when transferring skills to concept-similar scenarios. This work addresses three practical bottlenecks in deploying LLM skill libraries for legal adjudication—stale skills, semantic execution gaps, and context budget limits—which could make automated legal reasoning more reliable and maintainable over time. It matters for legal AI practitioners and anyone building continuously updated skill-based agent systems in high-stakes domains. The method combines three components: neighborhood-conditioned induction to refresh skills from recent data, execution-aligned refinement that analyzes LLM reasoning trajectories to close the gap between skill descriptions and intended expert logic, and context-budgeted routing to avoid omitting critical skills when collections grow. The reported 8.96% transfer improvement and consistent gains under continuous optimization suggest the generated skills are both transferable and stable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Liability adjudication tasks, such as determining legal charges, require analyzing event descriptions against domain-specific skills to decide whether a party bears liability. LLMs can use executable skills—structured, reusable workflows or prompt-based procedures—to produce interpretable reasoning, but skill libraries tend to become stale as real-world scenarios evolve, and skill descriptions often diverge from the expert logic they are meant to encode. As skill collections grow, they also exceed the model's context window, causing important skills to be dropped during selection.

<a id="item-294"></a>
## [TRAP: Trust-Responsive Runtime Authorization for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06996) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces the Trust Responsive Authorization Policy (TRAP), which maps an Authorization Readiness Score (ARS)—a weighted combination of runtime trust, operational stability, and contextual risk—onto discrete authorization levels using an asymmetric rule: escalation requires consecutive observations above a band edge plus a margin, while a sharp drop in readiness restricts authority immediately. On 100 Monte Carlo traces, TRAP is compared with static, trust-only, risk-only, and symmetric policies, showing faster restriction at onset and quicker re-escalation after recovery than the symmetric policy, with the highest and least variable stability among adaptive policies. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety: how to dynamically adjust an autonomous agent's operational authority based on runtime conditions rather than static rules. As agentic AI systems are deployed in high-stakes environments, such runtime authorization policies could become essential for balancing autonomy with safety, impacting developers and operators of AI agents. TRAP's restriction advantage depends on degradation being abrupt: in an exploratory experiment where degradation is spread over 5–20 intervals, TRAP restricts only on 4 traces that retain a single-interval drop above the threshold, and averaged over all 100 traces it holds authority above what readiness supports for 15.9–18.9 of the 20 degradation intervals, versus 0.8 under abrupt degradation. One-factor sweeps show recovery time rises with the escalation margin and run length, shifts with score weights, and is unchanged by the restriction threshold.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to autonomous systems that can take actions in digital or physical environments, such as calling APIs or modifying files. Runtime authorization is the process of deciding, during operation, what actions an agent is permitted to perform. Trust management involves estimating an agent's reliability and adjusting its permissions accordingly. Monte Carlo simulation is a method that uses repeated random sampling to evaluate the behavior of a policy under uncertainty.

<a id="item-295"></a>
## [EFXPLORER: Constrained Exploration for Cooperative MARL via Epigraph Reformulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07066) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose EFXPLORER, a constrained exploration framework for cooperative multi-agent reinforcement learning that maximizes exploration objectives subject to a constraint preserving task performance, solved via an epigraph reformulation with adaptive exploration budgets. It also incorporates a successor distance-based intrinsic reward to capture long-horizon dependencies, and outperforms strong baselines on SMAX, VMAS, and MPE benchmarks. This work addresses a key challenge in MARL: balancing exploration with task performance, as naive intrinsic rewards can distort learning signals. By separating intrinsic rewards from task objectives and regulating exploration through task feasibility, EFXPLORER offers a principled approach that could improve coordination discovery in complex multi-agent environments. The epigraph reformulation introduces adaptive exploration budgets, allowing the agent to explore as much as possible without violating the task performance constraint. The successor distance-based intrinsic reward encourages temporally extended exploration by capturing long-horizon state visitation patterns.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) involves multiple agents learning to cooperate in shared environments, but the combinatorial explosion of joint state-action spaces makes exploration difficult. Intrinsic rewards, such as curiosity or count-based bonuses, are often added to external rewards to encourage exploration, but they can interfere with learning the actual task. Constrained optimization techniques, like epigraph reformulation, convert constrained problems into simpler forms by introducing auxiliary variables, and are used here to manage exploration budgets.

## [Two-Stage Recommender Ranks Port Impacts from Tropical Cyclones Using AIS Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07296) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a two-stage recommendation framework that first retrieves candidate ports near a tropical cyclone's expected landfall and then ranks them by estimated impact days using AIS-derived data from 37 ports and 31 cyclones in the Gulf of Mexico. In tests on Hurricanes Nicole and Ian, the Wide & Deep ranking model achieved 85.5% and 55.5% better mean ranking agreement than a distance-based baseline, respectively. This work offers port authorities a data-driven tool to prioritize post-cyclone inspections and allocate recovery resources across multiple threatened ports, potentially improving disaster response efficiency. It also demonstrates how recommendation system techniques can be adapted to maritime logistics and climate risk management. The study compares three ways of representing port-cyclone interactions: pairwise feature interactions, tree-based feature combinations, and explicit feature crosses with learned embeddings. The retrieval stage consistently identified all observed affected ports under landfall uncertainty, and the Wide & Deep model was less sensitive to landfall location uncertainty than the baseline.

**Background**: Tropical cyclones can disrupt multiple ports simultaneously, but limited historical data and uncertain landfall locations make it hard to predict which ports will be hit hardest. AIS (Automatic Identification System) data, which tracks vessel movements, can be used to infer port activity and impact days. Two-stage recommendation systems, common in e-commerce, first retrieve a candidate set and then rank items by relevance, and here they are adapted to rank ports by expected cyclone impact.

## [DelegateEval: A New Framework for Evaluating Personal External Service Agent Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07508) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces DelegateEval, a platform-level evaluation framework with eight dimensions for assessing AI agent systems that let individuals delegate external client service work to personal agents. The eight dimensions are ease of creation, service discovery and matching, delivery quality, expertise inheritance, service continuity, learning and improvement, reliability and governance, and efficiency and value. This framework addresses a timely and underexplored class of AI agents—personal external service agents—that could enable individuals to serve more clients with less direct involvement and generate economic value at scale. It provides a structured taxonomy for platform builders and researchers to assess whether such platforms can turn expertise into ongoing, verifiable services. The framework is presented along with an experimental design, but complete datasets, executable environments, a unified scoring protocol, and cross-model results remain to be developed and empirically validated. As an arXiv preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review or community validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Personal external service agents are AI agents authorized and continuously managed by an individual to accept client requests, deliver work, and provide ongoing support using that person's knowledge, experience, professional methods, and service boundaries. Such agent systems may combine large language models, reusable skills, protocols such as MCP (Model Context Protocol), and executable tool environments. MCP is an open standard that enables secure, two-way connections between data sources and AI-powered tools, allowing agents to interact with external services. DelegateEval aims to evaluate platforms that support these agents across multiple dimensions, from creation to economic feasibility.

## [GNN-LBBD: Graph Learning-Assisted Benders Decomposition for Robust Train Rescheduling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07520) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces GNN-LBBD, a graph neural network-assisted logic-based Benders decomposition algorithm for distributionally robust train rescheduling under disruptions. On 28 test instances for a five-track station, it achieved a mean total completion delay of 61.13 train-min, which is 16.63% lower than tabu search and 20.06% lower than variable neighborhood search, with computation time close to tabu search. This work demonstrates that combining graph learning with decomposition-based optimization can yield significant improvements in delay reduction for railway disruption management, potentially leading to more efficient and reliable train operations. It also highlights the value of incorporating real-time disruption resolution observations into rescheduling decisions. The algorithm uses conditional cuts derived from resource-use patterns and a graph neural network to guide the order of solving resource relaxation subproblems. Information comparisons on six representative instances showed that using observations of disruption resolution reduced mean total completion delay by approximately 21.7%, with benefits more pronounced in instances with alternative routes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Distributionally robust optimization (DRO) is a framework that seeks decisions that perform well under the worst-case distribution within an ambiguity set of possible probability distributions, providing robustness against distributional uncertainty. Logic-based Benders decomposition (LBBD) is an optimization technique that partitions a problem into a master problem and subproblems, allowing arbitrary combinatorial structures and generating logic-based cuts instead of relying solely on linear relaxations. Graph neural networks (GNNs) are deep learning models designed to operate on graph-structured data, capturing dependencies between entities; here they are used to guide the solution process by predicting effective subproblem orders.

A new arXiv paper benchmarks four PII redaction designs—pure regex, Microsoft Presidio on spaCy, and two hybrids—on a resume-to-profile workflow using 100 synthetic resumes. Regex achieved 0.20 ms median latency but caught only 88.9% of PII, while Presidio, hybrid, and fast hybrid each caught 100% of PII and retained 100% of business terms at median latencies of 37.59 ms, 30.23 ms, and 13.48 ms respectively. This study provides practical latency and accuracy numbers for PII redaction, showing that even the slowest option adds only 0.41% to a 9,120 ms median LLM call, which can help teams justify adopting stronger redaction methods. It recommends the fast hybrid as a default for LLM pipelines handling personal data, with regex as a safety net rather than the sole control. All designs share a known-entity rule and a consistent placeholder scheme so the LLM can distinguish individuals after redaction. The evaluation used 100 synthetic resumes averaging 523 characters, containing 900 PII values and 600 business terms that must survive redaction.

**Background**: PII redaction removes personally identifiable information such as names, phone numbers, and email addresses from text before it is sent to an LLM, preventing data leakage into provider systems and logs. Regex is fast but limited to predefined patterns, while Microsoft Presidio is an open-source PII de-identification service that uses named entity recognition (NER) models like spaCy to detect entities such as person names. Hybrid designs combine regex for structured identifiers with NER for unstructured entities like names.

A new two-stage approach called generate-then-rank improves symbolic goal interpretation for embodied agents by first fine-tuning an LLM on the Embodied Agent Interface (EAI) output convention and then using Candidate-Output Ranking from Errors (CORE) to select the best output from the model's own generated alternatives. Experiments on household scenes show it outperforms baselines including simple prompting, chain-of-thought, and knowledge graph-based methods across open-source LLMs like DeepSeek-V3, DeepSeek-Reasoner, and Qwen2.5-7B/72B. Goal interpretation is a critical first step in embodied agent pipelines, as errors propagate to all downstream planning and action. This work offers a practical, model-agnostic improvement that could enhance the reliability of LLM-based embodied agents in real-world household tasks. The method is evaluated on the household scenes of the EAI benchmark, and CORE learns preferences over complete outputs from the model's own generated alternatives, which helps correct errors in the initial generation. The approach is tested on multiple open-source LLMs, demonstrating consistent improvements over strong baselines.

## [FARR: Forecast-Aware Residual Refinement for Long-Horizon Time-Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07870) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FARR introduces a forecast-aware residual refinement method that uses endpoint evidence from valid and null forecasts to correct long-horizon time-series predictions. Experiments on four benchmarks show it achieves the best dataset-average MSE and MAE among compared methods. Auxiliary conditioning in LLM-based forecasting does not always improve accuracy and can even increase error when poorly matched to temporal context. FARR's residual refinement approach offers a way to make conditioning more reliable, potentially benefiting any LLM-conditioned time-series model. FARR obtains a valid forecast under the standard condition and a null forecast under a predefined null condition, using their difference as evidence of how conditioning changes the prediction. A shared residual refiner combines this endpoint evidence with historical features to estimate the remaining error, and horizon-wise gating controls the residual's contribution to the final output.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Long-horizon time-series forecasting aims to predict many steps ahead, where errors accumulate and models must capture long-term dependencies. Recent work uses large language models (LLMs) to provide auxiliary conditioning—additional information such as text or context—to improve forecasts, but the influence of this conditioning can be misaligned with the observed temporal context. Residual learning, which models the error of a base predictor, is a common technique to refine forecasts.

## [Adaptive Semantic Branching: Training-Free Reasoning Candidate Expansion via Cross-Trajectory Disagreement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07918) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Adaptive Semantic Branching (ASB), a training-free inference procedure that uses disagreements between independently sampled reasoning trajectories to localize divergence points and generate targeted new candidate solutions. In a 500-problem MATH evaluation with Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, ASB increased oracle candidate coverage from 445/500 (89.0%) to 455/500 (91.0%), recovering correct candidates for 10 of the 55 initially uncovered problems. This work offers a lightweight, training-free way to improve the diversity and coverage of reasoning candidates without additional model training, which could be valuable for inference-time scaling and self-consistency methods in LLM reasoning. It also demonstrates how cross-trajectory disagreement can be exploited as a signal for targeted exploration. ASB groups sampled trajectories by final answer, aligns reasoning steps via a monotonic dynamic program, and identifies persistent divergence points to generate new solutions conditioned on a source prefix and a donor reasoning window. The diversity-aware policy allocates up to three additional generations per problem, using 576 extra generation calls across the evaluation set (averaging 8.152 calls per problem).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Reasoning in large language models often involves sampling multiple reasoning trajectories for the same problem, which may arrive at conflicting answers. These disagreements can reveal alternative intermediate decisions that are worth exploring. ASB builds on this idea by using disagreement as a signal to localize where reasoning paths diverge and to generate new candidate solutions from those points, without requiring any model training. The method relies on techniques like dynamic programming for sequence alignment and diversity-aware generation policies.

## [Circe: Training-Free LLM Self-Evaluation via Component Interaction Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07948) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Circe introduces a training-free self-evaluation method that combines internal component interaction consistency (CIC) with representation geometry (RG) and a bounded likelihood confidence (LC) score to assess LLM response correctness without reference answers or repeated sampling. Experiments on five LLMs and six benchmarks across four domains show it outperforms existing baselines and generalizes to multilingual, multimodal, and open-world settings. Reliable evaluation of LLM outputs without reference answers is critical for real-world deployment, and Circe's use of internal computation signals offers a complementary approach to output-based methods that can be fooled by incorrect but fluent responses. This could improve trust and safety in applications where ground truth is unavailable. CIC measures directional alignment between attention updates in earlier layers and attention-induced FFN output changes in later layers, while RG characterizes normalized displacement and angular changes along hidden-state trajectories; these are fused with LC derived from mean negative log-likelihood. The method requires no training or multiple sampling, but relies on access to internal model states and may be sensitive to architecture-specific patterns.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Large language models often produce fluent but incorrect responses, and in practice reference answers are rarely available for evaluation. Existing reference-free methods mainly use output signals like likelihood or self-consistency, but these can assign high scores to wrong answers. Recent work explores internal hidden-state trajectories across layers, but typically overlooks how attention and feed-forward network components interact, which Circe aims to address.

## [Secure Formal Raising framework enforces security invariants in AI development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08042) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Secure Formal Raising (SFR), a framework that models policy integrity, authorization, and confidential data flow using four-valued evidence that preserves unknown and inconsistent assessments. It proves six security invariants and provides a plain-C reference implementation that satisfies them in a bounded exhaustive exploration, while also exposing a confused-deputy gap for tool calls that a provenance requirement closes. This work brings formal security guarantees to AI development environments, which are increasingly complex and vulnerable to privilege escalation and data leaks. By proving invariants and providing a reference implementation, it offers a rigorous foundation for building trustworthy AI systems, potentially influencing future secure AI tooling and standards. The four-valued evidence logic preserves unknown and inconsistent assessments, and the six proven invariants force admission to accept only confirmed evidence. The reference implementation is in plain C and was validated through bounded exhaustive exploration, which also revealed a confused-deputy gap that is closed by adding a provenance requirement for tool calls.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Model raising is a paradigm that treats AI development like raising a child, integrating alignment from the start rather than after training. Formal Raising (FR) extends this by adding explicit behavioral properties. Secure Formal Raising (SFR) focuses on the security of the raising environment. The confused deputy problem is a classic security vulnerability where a privileged program is tricked into misusing its authority, often mitigated by capability-based security. Four-valued logic extends Boolean logic to handle unknown and inconsistent information, useful for reasoning under uncertainty.

## [Small Startup Cuts AI Costs with Hybrid Self-Hosted Stack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08116) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Grayhat Developers, a small software company, reported deploying a hybrid AI stack that combines commercial coding agents like Claude/Codex for complex tasks with local open-source models for simpler work, after finding that fully local models lacked sufficient performance and speed. The company estimates that moving 30–40% of its commercial AI workload—mainly context transfer, documentation sync, and task management—to local models could reduce overall commercial AI usage by 27–36%. This practical experience report provides concrete cost data and lessons for small teams considering self-hosting AI, showing that local inference is most valuable as part of a hybrid architecture rather than a wholesale replacement for commercial AI. It highlights that UX friction, not just model capability, can determine whether an internal AI deployment is adopted. The initial fully local deployment failed due to UX friction, but a centrally configured deployment substantially improved feedback and adoption. The company integrated local models with self-hosted tools including Outline (wiki), Plane (project management), and ERPNext (ERP), and notes that commercial AI usage was about $325 per month with over 25.93 billion recorded tokens.

## [SMART: Multi-Agent Framework Curates State Knowledge to Boost Claim Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08196) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SMART is a multi-agent framework that captures the implicit state of entities in a problem context to curate diverse, complementary, and solution-salient premises using multiple LLMs and a structured role-based prompt strategy. It achieves up to 20% performance gains on complex scientific claim verification across three datasets, with grounding premises in real-world knowledge adding up to 5% improvement. This work demonstrates that multi-agent collaboration with structured knowledge curation can significantly enhance LLM reasoning for verification tasks, which is crucial for scientific fact-checking and trustworthy AI. The approach could be adapted to other domains requiring complex reasoning with limited context, impacting researchers and practitioners in AI and scientific publishing. The framework uses a role-based prompt strategy to extract state knowledge from multiple LLMs, curating premises that are diverse, complementary, and salient for solution reasoning. Grounding these premises in real-world verified knowledge is shown to be necessary, contributing up to 5% of the total performance gain.

Multi-agent systems (MAS) extend LLM reasoning by having multiple agents collaborate, but most frameworks aggregate outputs via simple majority voting, which can be brittle. SMART addresses this by curating structured state knowledge from multiple LLMs to guide solution reasoning, rather than just voting on final answers.

## [Interpretable RL for wind farm power control via policy distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08245) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a policy distillation framework that uses explainable boosting machines (EBMs) to convert a reinforcement learning policy into a transparent controller for wind farm implicit balancing. Using real data from the Belgian grid and an offshore wind farm, the resulting value-oriented and expert-based controller reduces imbalance costs by 82% and outperforms both a price threshold controller and a decision-tree distillation baseline. Reinforcement learning could improve wind farm participation in imbalance settlement, but its black-box nature blocks deployment on real farms; this work shows that a glass-box distilled policy can retain strong economic performance while remaining justifiable to stakeholders. It offers a practical path toward trustworthy AI in energy system operations, where transparency and regulatory acceptance are essential. EBMs are tree-based, cyclic gradient-boosting generalized additive models with automatic interaction detection, and the paper introduces a value-oriented perspective to policy distillation inspired by energy forecasting research, while also incorporating expert knowledge during model design. The abstract is truncated, so full details on the RL algorithm, state/action design, and evaluation scope are not available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Implicit balancing lets a wind farm adjust its power setpoint in anticipation of imbalance prices published by the transmission system operator, helping restore grid balance while reducing its own imbalance costs from forecast errors. Because the settlement price is unknown in advance and affected by the control strategy itself, reinforcement learning is a natural fit, but standard RL policies are opaque. Policy distillation trains a simpler, interpretable model to mimic the RL policy, and explainable boosting machines are a glass-box model class that remains accurate while being fully interpretable.

## [StepBack: Step-Level Error-Localized Resampling for Efficient Test-Time Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08403) ⭐️ 6.0/10

StepBack introduces a step-level error-localized resampling method that, given a small set of draft reasoning trajectories, uses lightweight signals to identify likely first error positions and then resamples only the detected error step and its suffix before majority voting. Across seven mathematical reasoning and multi-hop QA benchmarks, it matches self-consistency accuracy on average while reducing token usage by up to 41.26%. This work addresses the high inference cost of self-consistency, a widely used decoding strategy for chain-of-thought reasoning, by avoiding full-trajectory resampling and focusing computation on error-prone steps. It offers a practical efficiency improvement for test-time reasoning and can be combined with adaptive inference methods like Adaptive Consistency to further reduce cost. StepBack relies on lightweight signals to localize the first likely error step, then resamples that step and its suffix rather than entire trajectories. The reported 41.26% token reduction is an upper bound observed across the seven benchmarks, and the method is designed to be model-agnostic and composable with adaptive inference techniques.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Self-consistency is a decoding strategy that improves chain-of-thought reasoning by sampling many complete reasoning paths and taking a majority vote over their final answers, but it is expensive because each path is generated in full. Adaptive Consistency reduces cost by dynamically adjusting the number of samples per question using a lightweight stopping criterion. Test-time compute scaling has become a central lever for improving LLM reasoning, making efficient sampling methods increasingly important.

## [Paper Proposes 'Meta Access' Ladder for LLM Chat Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08428) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.08428) introduces 'meta access,' a structured and permissioned way for LLM assistants to view the chat interface itself, and proposes a five-level ladder from feature tutoring to full orchestration, demonstrated via a Meta Chat prototype in the Chat AI client. This work could transform chat clients from simple conversation windows into control layers for entire workspaces, extending chat toward agentic capability while preserving user control, and may influence how AI-era interfaces expose their state and capabilities. The ladder includes feature tutoring; metadata, settings, and preference access; retrieval over summaries and messages; confirmed mutation; and full orchestration. The paper highlights risks around grounding, privacy, prompt injection, and trust, and argues that because general-purpose agentic harnesses can change their own settings, meta access becomes reflexive and requires a separate control-plane boundary.

**Background**: Large language model assistants typically see only the current dialogue, not the surrounding interface state such as settings, conversation history, or workspace organization. Agentic harnesses are runtime layers that constrain an AI agent's execution, and control-plane boundaries separate the agent's actions from the systems it manages.

## [Dynamical model formalizes AI's positive and negative effects on human cognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06928) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.06928) presents a phenomenological dynamical model that formalizes how cognitive offloading to generative AI can both augment and degrade human fluid and crystallized intelligence. The model extends the bivariate latent change score formalism from cognitive-aging research by adding a fast machine-intelligence variable, and uses singular-perturbation reduction to identify saddle-node and Hopf bifurcations that correspond to AI-induced cognitive decline and augmentation. As generative AI becomes ubiquitous, concerns about cognitive offloading and long-term decline in unassisted reasoning are growing; this model offers a formal framework to reason about when AI use might augment versus erode human cognitive abilities. It could inform future empirical studies and policy discussions about responsible AI deployment in education and knowledge work. The model uses Hill-function modifications to couple machine intelligence with human self-feedback and cross-coupling coefficients, and a Tikhonov-justified singular-perturbation reduction collapses the three-dimensional system onto a two-dimensional slow manifold. The authors emphasize that the model is phenomenological with limited empirical validation, and they state conditions under which each dynamical regime applies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: The Cattell-Horn-Carroll (CHC) theory distinguishes fluid intelligence (Gf), the capacity for novel reasoning, from crystallized intelligence (Gc), accumulated knowledge. Bivariate latent change score models are statistical tools used in cognitive-aging research to study how Gf and Gc change together over time. Hill functions are sigmoidal nonlinearities commonly used in dynamical systems modeling, for example in gene regulatory networks, to represent saturating responses.

## [ApproxBrain Certifies Sufficient Neural Circuits with Bounded Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06929) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces a formal framework that defines computational sufficiency as a cost-indexed family of neuron subsets, conditioned on a task and tolerance pair (epsilon, rho), and certifies candidates using one-sided bounds on held-out parameter draws. Applied to a released walking simulator, it certifies the published 148-neuron circuit as sufficient with margin, shows no 146- or 147-neuron subset is sufficient, and demonstrates that removing any of its three interneurons destroys adequacy. This work shifts the study of compact neural circuits from point estimates to set-valued, certified frontiers, providing a more rigorous way to assess which neurons are necessary for a computation. It could influence how neuroscientists interpret connectome-constrained models and how they design experiments to test circuit sufficiency. The certification uses 32 held-out parameter draws with a mean task degradation of 0.017 and no failures, and the framework also measures non-decomposability, degeneracy, and an intervention-derived role taxonomy. Results show that sufficiency is set-valued and depends jointly on task and tolerance, with no single search strategy dominating across regimes, and all findings are model-relative.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: A connectome is a comprehensive map of neural connections in an organism, such as the worm C. elegans or the fruit fly, often obtained via electron microscopy. Computational sufficiency refers to whether a given set of neurons can support a specific computation, and previous studies often reported a single minimal circuit as a point estimate. Degeneracy, the ability of different neural circuits to perform the same function, complicates the identification of sufficient circuits.

A new arXiv paper (2611.06936) synthesizes historical critiques of computational functionalism—from Korzybski's map-territory distinction to Lerchner's recent 'abstraction fallacy'—to argue for a non-computational physicalism where consciousness depends on metabolically grounded physical dynamics rather than abstract computation. This paper contributes to the philosophy of mind and AI consciousness debates by challenging the dominant computational functionalist view, which has implications for whether artificial consciousness is possible and how we interpret AI systems. The paper addresses the objection that its critique targets an outdated computationalism by arguing that even mechanistic computationalism (Milkowski, Piccinini, Craver, Bechtel, Kaplan) still depends on observer-relative choices of state individuation and system boundaries, so the abstraction fallacy persists.

## [Frozen Forecasting Models Barely Beat Strong Baselines on MONSTER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06988) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A controlled benchmark evaluates two forecasting foundation models, Reverso and Chronos-Bolt, as frozen feature extractors for time series classification on six MONSTER datasets. Pretraining improves accuracy over random-weight versions on five of six datasets (up to 23 percentage points), but frozen representations fail to reliably outperform the strongest non-pretrained classifier, losing in 15 of 17 settings and tying in two. This finding challenges the assumption that pretrained forecasting representations are universally superior for downstream classification, urging practitioners to include strong task-specific baselines and random-weight controls. It affects how time series foundation models are evaluated and adopted in classification tasks, especially when labeled data are scarce. The study uses 1%, 10%, and 100% of training labels and compares against four established time series classification methods. Restoring amplitude information recovers part of the performance gap, while fine-tuning and forecast-error features do not close it.

**Background**: Time series foundation models like Reverso and Chronos-Bolt are pretrained on large corpora of time series to perform zero-shot forecasting. MONSTER is a benchmark suite of large time series datasets used to evaluate such models.

## [JPTA: Prior-Aware Framework for Robust Transductive Adaptation of VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06993) ⭐️ 6.0/10

JPTA (Joint Prior and Transport Adaptation) is a new prior-aware framework that treats CLIP text prototypes as batch-conditioned semantic references that shift toward image-side batch structure only under calibrated evidence. It estimates batch priors and soft image-side prototypes to form transported semantic references whose impact is scaled by batch reliability, and consistently outperforms TransCLIP and StatA across standard benchmarks, low effective-class regimes, all-class evaluation, and online streams. This work addresses a key trade-off in transductive VLM adaptation between aggressive methods that exploit batch structure but drift under sparse or prior-shifted batches and conservative methods that preserve CLIP semantics but under-adapt. By recalibrating semantic references using reliable batch evidence, JPTA offers a more robust approach that could improve test-time adaptation of vision-language models in real-world deployment scenarios with distribution shift. JPTA introduces diagnostics such as anchor-drift, active-support, and absent-class-mass to show that it fixes effective-class mismatch without unconstrained transductive drift. The method is evaluated against TransCLIP and StatA, with the largest gains on sparse and prior-shifted batches, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Transductive adaptation improves vision-language models like CLIP at test time by refining predictions on an unlabeled batch of test data, leveraging the structure of that batch. CLIP text prototypes are the text embeddings of class names that serve as a zero-shot classifier, and prior shift refers to a change in the label distribution between training and test data. Existing methods either aggressively exploit batch structure, risking drift when batches are sparse or shifted, or conservatively keep fixed text prototypes, limiting adaptation.

## [GENIQ: Training-Free Skin Lesion Segmentation via Text-to-Image Generator's Own Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07061) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose GENIQ, a training-free method that uses a text-to-image diffusion transformer to generate healthy-skin and lesion images, from which it constructs prototypes for skin lesion segmentation. The key insight is that the direction derived from generated images is better aligned with real skin-lesion separation than word-based attention directions, achieving Dice scores of 0.85-0.88 on three dermoscopy test sets without annotated masks. This work demonstrates a novel way to leverage generative models for medical image segmentation without any training or annotations, potentially reducing the reliance on costly expert-labeled data. It could benefit dermatology screening in resource-limited settings where annotated dermoscopy datasets are scarce. GENIQ refines initial prototypes using the most skin-like and lesion-like patches from unlabelled dermoscopy images, scores test patches along the refined direction, and applies a gated threshold to suppress contextual spill-over around small lesions. It outperforms zero-shot and word-based attention methods on all three sets and a hand-designed dermoscopy pipeline on two.

## [SD-LoRA Distills Graph Reasoning into LoRA for Few-Shot CLIP Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07063) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SD-LoRA introduces a training-time structural distillation framework that uses a heterogeneous graph teacher over high-resolution ViT tokens to model patch-patch, global-local, and visual-text relations, then transfers this structural bias into a lightweight LoRA-adapted CLIP model via implicit distillation and Prototype Predictive Alignment. At inference, the graph teacher is discarded, requiring only a single CLIP-LoRA forward pass and a cache-based classifier, and across 11 few-shot benchmarks it improves over a strong CLIP-LoRA baseline by 2.4/1.8/3.0/2.4/2.0 percentage points in the 1/2/4/8/16-shot settings, respectively. This work shows that token-level structural supervision can be injected only during training and then fully removed at test time, offering a practical way to improve few-shot vision-language adaptation without adding inference overhead. It could benefit deployment scenarios where frozen CLIP-like models are preferred for efficiency but fine-grained distinctions from limited data are needed. The graph teacher operates only during training and is discarded at inference, so prediction requires just one CLIP-LoRA forward pass plus a cache-based classifier; the method also reports gains on base-to-novel generalization, cross-dataset transfer, out-of-distribution robustness, and calibration. Code is available at the provided GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: CLIP is a vision-language model trained contrastively on image-text pairs, and its frozen image and text encoders are widely used for zero-shot and few-shot classification. LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adds small trainable low-rank matrices to selected layers while keeping the original model weights frozen. Knowledge distillation typically trains a smaller student model to mimic a larger teacher, and here the teacher is a graph-based module used only during training to provide structural guidance.

## [One-Step Latent Score Can Replace Lookahead in Proactive Information Acquisition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07155) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2611.07155) argues that a one-step score on a suitably chosen latent cause can replace expensive lookahead in proactive information acquisition, provided the latent is kept small and validated against exact values. The key finding is that the need for depth often lies in how the hidden cause is described, not in the planning itself. This work could significantly reduce the computational cost of proactive agents that must decide what to ask before acting, making them more practical for real-time applications. It also highlights the importance of representation design in reinforcement learning and active learning, suggesting that better latent descriptions can substitute for deeper search. The paper shows that a one-step score on the label can be blind to interactions among answers, while a score on a suitable latent cause is not; however, the same problem described differently can defeat the score even though exact values do not move. It also notes that probabilities learned from one language model's answers do not transfer to another unless planning allows for both, and those read from tokens are worth only a few labels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Proactive information acquisition is a sequential decision problem where an agent chooses questions to maximize information gain before acting, often formalized with value of information (VOI) and lookahead search. Exact lookahead evaluates all combinations of questions but is computationally expensive and relies on a fitted model, while one-step scores are cheap but may miss important interactions. Latent variable models aim to capture hidden causes behind observations, and the choice of latent representation can critically affect decision-making performance.

The paper introduces EANK (Evolutionary Adaptive Neighborhood K), a method that uses a real-coded elitist genetic algorithm to optimize a low-dimensional policy mapping local neighborhood descriptors (density, label entropy, vote margin, concentration) to an instance-specific number of neighbors k(x). Experiments on synthetic data and twelve public datasets show that under nested cross-validation, EANK is competitive with tuned global-k baselines and improves upon the default k=5. It offers a practical approach for improving k-NN performance without exhaustive search, potentially benefiting classification tasks where local structure matters. The genetic algorithm optimizes the policy using leave-one-out accuracy with a neighborhood-smoothness penalty, and precomputed neighbor votes reduce fitness evaluation costs. The paper also provides theoretical analysis on dominance over global-k solutions, evolutionary convergence, policy regularity, and generalization guarantees.

**Background**: The k-nearest neighbor (k-NN) classifier is a simple, widely used algorithm that predicts the label of a query point based on the majority vote of its k nearest neighbors. The choice of k controls the bias-variance trade-off: small k leads to low bias but high variance, while large k leads to high bias but low variance. ## [Satisficing reframed as Bayesian weighting with unattained goals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07270) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper shows that Risk-sensitive Satisficing (RS), a bandit learning rule, can be rewritten as a Bayesian-like estimator called Attention Competitive Bayes (ACB), where the aspiration level serves as the prior mean for every action and the finite trial budget controls how strongly evidence can override it. This reformulation yields two consequences: actions no longer sampled are partially pulled toward the goal (pseudo-forgetting), and when the aspiration is unattainable, exploration does not settle, but adjusting the aspiration from evidence reduced regret in Bernoulli bandit simulations. This work bridges decision theory and cognitive science by providing a formal, analyzable model of how unattained goals constrain choice, which could inform reinforcement learning algorithms and psychological theories of goal-directed behavior. It offers testable predictions about how organisms revalue abandoned options, making it relevant to researchers in both fields. The ACB model distinguishes itself from models with an explicit forgetting rate because its pseudo-forgetting arises from the goal acting as a prior rather than from discarding data. The analysis is limited to a minimal bandit setting, and the regret reduction was demonstrated in simulations with Bernoulli bandits, so empirical validation in more complex environments remains to be done.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Satisficing is a decision strategy where an agent seeks an option that meets an aspiration level rather than maximizing expected reward. Risk-sensitive Satisficing (RS) is a bandit learning rule that values actions by their selection ratio multiplied by surplus or shortfall relative to the aspiration level. The paper draws on Terrence Deacon's concept of ententionality, which describes how goals not yet realized can organize behavior, and on the idea that ends act as constraints rather than particular states.

## [LLM Agent Uses Learned Router for Selective Retrieval in Knowledge Graph Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07342) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes a selective retrieval routing framework for multi-violation knowledge graph repair, where an LLM repair agent edits the graph and a trained router decides when to access external evidence via a researcher agent. It extends a violation-injection framework to generate cases with multiple compatible violations and known reference repairs, using the agent's outcomes to train deployment-specific routers. This work addresses the costly and unreliable nature of external retrieval in graph repair by learning when to retrieve, showing that selective access can outperform both always-on and always-off retrieval strategies in token efficiency and repair F1. It has implications for building more autonomous and efficient LLM-based data cleaning systems, particularly in domain-specific deployments like building automation. On two building-automation datasets, researcher access improved repairs for some violations but degraded them for others, with effects varying across deployments. The router used fewer repair-time tokens than always enabling access in all six evaluation suites and achieved higher repair F1 than always disabling access in five of six suites.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs often contain missing or inconsistent facts, and schema validation can detect violations but not determine the intended repair. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) allows LLMs to incorporate external evidence, but retrieval is costly and not always successful. This paper introduces a router that selectively triggers retrieval during iterative graph repair by an LLM agent, trained using outcomes from cases with multiple violations.

<a id="item-323"></a>
## [FreePath: Neural Mean Free Path for Image Dehazing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07398) ⭐️ 6.0/10

FreePath introduces a neural mean free path framework that represents single image dehazing as feature space path state restoration, guided by learned path and collision fields within a residual encoder-decoder. Experiments on CDD-11 and RTTS datasets show consistent improvements in full-reference fidelity, no-reference perceptual quality, and visual faithfulness over representative baselines. This physics-inspired approach offers an alternative to unstable explicit scattering inversion and direct mapping methods, potentially improving robustness in complex scenes for computer vision applications like autonomous driving and surveillance. It bridges physical haze formation modeling with deep feature learning, which could influence future dehazing research. The Mean Free Path Residual Block learns path and collision fields to guide haze-constrained correction, while FreePath State Conservation Integration preserves restored path states during deep feature aggregation. The method is evaluated on paired CDD-11 haze images and real RTTS scenes, but details on computational cost and generalization to other haze types are not provided in the abstract.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Single image dehazing aims to recover clear scene radiance from hazy images, which is challenging due to unknown scene radiance and transmission. Traditional physics-based methods invert explicit scattering variables but can be unstable, while data-driven methods learn direct mappings but may discard physical organization. The mean free path concept, borrowed from physics, describes the average distance a particle travels before colliding, and here it inspires a neural framework to model haze effects in feature space.

## [PSGAgent uses agentic reasoning to boost MLLM visual perception for scene graph generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07408) ⭐️ 6.0/10

PSGAgent introduces an agentic think-act-observe framework that equips Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) with a Region Zoom-In Tool and a Visual Highlight Tool to dynamically refine visual perception for panoptic scene graph generation (PSG). The method uses a two-stage training pipeline with a Multimodal Chain-of-Thought dataset and a hierarchical reward mechanism integrated with Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), achieving state-of-the-art results on the PSG benchmark. This work addresses a key limitation of MLLMs—their fixed-resolution perception—which causes them to miss small or peripheral objects and confuse ambiguous predicates in complex scenes. By enabling dynamic, tool-based visual exploration, PSGAgent could improve fine-grained scene understanding in applications like robotics, autonomous driving, and image retrieval, and it demonstrates how agentic reasoning can enhance multimodal models beyond static inference. The framework uses a two-stage training pipeline: first, a high-quality, error-aware Multimodal Chain-of-Thought (M-CoT) dataset for cold-start learning; second, a hierarchical reward mechanism with GSPO to optimize strategic tool usage. Experiments on the PSG benchmark show state-of-the-art performance, though the paper is an arXiv preprint and details like computational cost and generalization to other tasks are not yet fully explored.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Panoptic scene graph generation (PSG) is a task that requires models to parse an image into a structured graph of objects (with panoptic segmentation masks) and their pairwise relationships, enabling holistic yet fine-grained scene understanding. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have been applied to PSG but typically process images at a fixed resolution, acting as static observers that miss small or peripheral details. Agentic reasoning frameworks, such as the think-act-observe loop, allow models to iteratively plan, take actions (e.g., call tools), and observe results, which has proven effective in various AI agent systems.

## [Eigenius: Dependent Type Theory for Typed Knowledge with Proof Provenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07432) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Eigenius, a dependent type theory designed as a persistent store of typed knowledge that retains proof witnesses and provenance, using an orthogonal rewriting system for definitional equality and treating proof irrelevance as an axiom with explicit witnesses rather than a conversion rule. This work addresses a gap in existing type theories, which typically erase proof provenance via proof irrelevance, and could impact the design of knowledge representation systems and proof assistants that need to track evidence and assumptions. The kernel includes cumulative universes, an impredicative universe of propositions, inductive families, and an intensional identity type; consistency is proved via a set-theoretic model, and a Lean 4 formalization accompanies the volume.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Dependent type theory is a foundation for proof assistants like Lean, Rocq, and Agda, where types can depend on values. Proof irrelevance is a principle that all proofs of a proposition are equal, often built into conversion, which erases how a proof was constructed. Definitional equality determines when two terms are equal by computation, and rewriting systems provide a way to decide it.

## [New Regularization Method Suppresses Error Accumulation in Time-Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07473) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces local rollout consistency, a training objective that penalizes the Jacobian-vector product of the predictor along feedback perturbations to reduce error accumulation in time-series forecasting. The method uses a first-order surrogate to avoid designing a residual-to-state alignment operator, and code is released on GitHub. This work addresses a fundamental mismatch in autoregressive forecasting: models trained with teacher forcing often degrade when fed their own predictions. The proposed regularization offers a practical way to improve long-horizon accuracy without architectural changes, potentially benefiting any sequential predictor. The method penalizes the Jacobian-vector product along the actual feedback perturbation, targeting directions where self-generated errors remain visible to future predictions. A geometric interpretation shows that controlling residual-induced directions probing the normal bundle yields conditions for free-running trajectories to stay within a stability tube around the predictive manifold.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: In time-series forecasting, autoregressive models are often trained with teacher forcing, where ground-truth values are fed as inputs at each step. At inference, however, the model must run freely using its own predictions, and small errors can compound over time. Rollout consistency aims to align training with this free-running regime, but directly enforcing it is challenging when state and output dimensions differ. Jacobian-vector products, efficiently computed via forward-mode automatic differentiation, measure how a function's output changes along a given direction.

## [VLM Monitor Detects Semantic Anomalies in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07540) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2611.07540) proposes a two-stage Vision-Language Model (VLM) monitor that first predicts the scene-required action from an outcome-hidden video window and then compares it with the actual ego-motion trajectory to flag semantic anomalies. The authors also contribute a six-field taxonomy of 112 paired scenarios (42 from documented incidents) and a generated video corpus, reporting 83.0% accuracy and 0.773 anomaly recall on 235 clips. This work addresses a key gap in AV safety monitoring by focusing on action appropriateness rather than mere visual unusualness, which could lead to more reliable anomaly detection systems. It also provides a structured taxonomy and dataset that may spur further research in VLM-based safety monitors for autonomous driving. The two-stage protocol with a fixed T-2.5 s window achieves 83.0% accuracy and 0.773 anomaly recall, compared to 74.5% and 0.536 for a single-stage video-only baseline, though specificity drops from 0.928 to 0.880. The results are preliminary and based on a generated video corpus, so real-world validation remains future work.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual perception with language understanding and are increasingly used in autonomous driving for scene understanding and planning, as seen in systems like DriveVLM and VLM-AD. Semantic anomaly detection in autonomous driving aims to identify situations that are unusual or unsafe, but data scarcity and unreliable VLM performance have hindered practical deployment. This paper introduces action-conditioned monitoring, where the system checks whether the ego vehicle's action is appropriate for the scene, rather than just detecting visual oddities.

## [Energy-Aware Open-Vocabulary Detection Scheduling Boosts Robot Mission Success](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07663) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present an energy-aware scheduler that dynamically switches YOLO-World-S among low-, mid-, and high-power modes on a Jetson Orin Nano using odometry, map change, and detector confidence. In 40 real navigation trials on an AgileX LIMO robot, the scheduler achieved 8/10 mission success versus 7/10 for fixed-rate baselines, while reducing mean Jetson input power by 5.2% and lowering OVD call rate by 34.0% compared to an 8 Hz high-rate baseline. This work addresses the inefficiency of fixed-rate open-vocabulary detection on power-constrained mobile robots, showing that robot-state-aware scheduling can improve task reliability while saving power. It offers a practical approach for edge robotics where compute and energy budgets are tight, potentially extending mission durations and enabling more autonomous operation. The scheduler does not minimize mission-window Jetson input energy because longer missions can erase instantaneous power savings; a separate 60 s stationary microbenchmark showed mean power dropping from 8.045 W to 7.301 W and energy from 484.7 J to 438.1 J relative to the 8 Hz baseline. The evaluation used two target objects (bottle and cup) with five placements per target-policy pair and four perception policies.

**Background**: Open-vocabulary object detection (OVD) allows robots to detect objects described by text queries at runtime, but running detection at a fixed high rate wastes energy on uninformative frames. ## [Parallel Query Complexity of Weakly Convex Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07675) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper establishes tight bounds on the number of parallel query rounds needed to find approximate stationary points of nonsmooth weakly convex functions, showing that the standard subgradient rate is optimal in high dimensions even for randomized algorithms with polynomially many parallel queries per round. It also shows that exploiting the order structure of the real line enables a deterministic algorithm using only function values to achieve logarithmic round complexity, with matching lower bounds even when each query returns the entire subdifferential. This work clarifies the role of dimension in determining the benefits of parallel queries for weakly convex optimization, providing theoretical guidance for when parallelization can significantly accelerate optimization algorithms. It impacts the design of parallel and distributed optimization methods, particularly for large-scale machine learning problems involving nonsmooth nonconvex objectives. The results are tight in the stated parameter regime, with matching lower bounds that hold even when each query returns the entire subdifferential. The logarithmic round complexity is achieved by a deterministic algorithm that uses only function values, and parallel queries can further reduce the round complexity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Nov 6, 05:00

**Background**: Weakly convex functions are a class of nonconvex functions that are convex up to a quadratic term, and they often arise in machine learning and signal processing. The Moreau envelope is a smoothing technique that transforms a nonsmooth function into a continuously differentiable one, allowing the use of gradient-based methods. Parallel query complexity measures how many rounds of parallel queries are needed to find an approximate stationary point, which is relevant for distributed and parallel computing environments.
